1. Infrastructure De-anonymization & Misconfigurations
Problem Statement Component Addressed:
Here are the links to the research papers, standards, and technical resources mentioned in your
text.
Research Papers
Tools & Technical Resources
Infrastructure & Reconnaissance
"Onion Services in the Wild: A Study of Deanonymization Attacks"
This paper investigates the practical aspects of deanonymizing Onion Service users and
operators (Tippe & Tippe, 2024).
Read the paper
"Identifying Similar Users Between Dark Web and Surface Webs using BERTopic and
Authorship Attribution"
This recent study tackles dark web persona matching across different platforms (Shin et
al., 2025).
Read the paper
"Writeprints: A Promising Method for Authorship Attribution for Cybercrime
Investigations"
The foundational paper referenced here was actually published under the title 
"Writeprints: A stylometric approach to identity-level identification and similarity detection
in cyberspace" by Abbasi and Chen.
Read the paper via ACM
"Bitcoin and Darknet Markets: Transaction Graph Analysis"
An exact academic paper published with this title does not exist in standard academic
databases; it likely acts as a descriptive heading for the general body of literature and
heuristic guides surrounding blockchain address clustering (such as the common-input
ownership heuristic).
OnionScan:GitHub Repository (Originally developed by Sarah Jamie Lewis)
Censys:Search Engine | Developer Documentation

1. Infrastructure De-anonymization & Origin Server Discovery
Problem Statement Component:
Identifying misconfigurations in Tor hidden services (exposed status pages, SSL certificates,
default banners, descriptor leaks) and correlating them to clearnet infrastructure to unmask
origin backend IPs.
Resource What It Reveals About
the Problem Statement
Concrete Artifacts to Extract & Implement
OnionScan(Lewis) Hidden services are
rarely deanonymized
via cryptographic
breaks in Tor; they fail
because standard web
servers (Apache,
Nginx) assume
clearnet defaults and
leak server metadata.
• Module: Automated HTTP probing
routines for /server-status,
/server-info, .git/, and .env.
• Regex Parsers: Extract client IP tables
and virtual host entries exposed inside
Apache mod_status.
• Header Extractors: Scrape deterministic
response headers (ETag,
Last-Modified, precise server version
strings).
Censys & Shodan
Docs
Origin servers co-host
clearnet and darknet
endpoints on the same
IP or reuse identical
cryptographic and
static assets across
both interfaces.
• Asset Fingerprints: SHA-256 hashes of
SSL/TLS certificates and MurmurHash3
algorithms for favicon.ico.
• JARM Signatures: Generate 62-character
TLS client/server handshake fingerprints to
identify unique server configurations.
• Search Queries: Automated API scripts
querying Censys/Shodan via:
services.tls.certificates.leaf_data
<SHA-256>
or http.favicon.hash:<MurmurHash>.
"Onion Services in the
Wild"(Tippe & Tippe,
2024)
Empirically documents
real-world operational
security (OpSec)
failures that lead to
• Detection Rules: Methods to identify
Server Name Indication (SNI) mismatches
and TLS certificate common names leaking
clearnet FQDNs.

edges.

RFC 4880(OpenPGP
Standard)
Threat actors switch handles
frequently across
marketplaces, but their
master PGP keys remain their
primary cryptographic
identity and reputation
anchor.

full 160-bit SHA-1 / 256-bit

spoofable 8-byte Key IDs).

signature creation timestamps,

strings (

merge rule: If Actor_A on
Market 1 and Actor_B on
Market 2 share an identical
Master PGP Fingerprint, merge
their entities into a unified
ActorProfile.

Problem Statement Component:
Using AI analysis, stylometric persona identification, and behavioral profiling to link rebranded
or migrated personas to known threat actors when all cryptographic indicators have been
discarded.

Neo4j Graph Academy Flat relational tables fail to
reveal multi-hop actor
associations (e.g., Actor A
transferred funds to Wallet B,

PGP Key C).

- Nodes:ThreatActor,
ForumHandle, PGPKey,

USES_KEY, DEPOSITED_TO,
POSTED_ON,
TRANSFERRED_FUNDS_TO.

traversal queries to detect
circular fund transfers, shared
escrow accounts, and wallet
consolidation hubs.

=== PAGE 6 ===
Resource What It Reveals About
the Problem Statement
Concrete Artifacts to Extract &
Implement
"Writeprints"(Abbasi &
Chen)
An author's
subconscious linguistic
habits (lexical richness,
punctuation patterns,
whitespace habits)
persist across
pseudonyms and
account resets.
• Stylometric Feature Vector (400+
features):
- Lexical: Yule’s K, Simpson’s D
(vocabulary richness), Hapax Legomena
ratios.
- Character/Orthographic: Punctuation
density (!, ?, ellipses), capitalization
frequency, special character
distributions.
- Structural: Paragraph count,
indentation quirks, greeting/sign-off
styles, bullet patterns.
Hugging Face /
Siamese Networks
Raw feature counting
struggles with very
short forum posts; deep
sentence embeddings
trained contrastively
capture semantic and
syntactic context
simultaneously.
• Verification Pipeline: A fine-tuned
transformer (e.g., RoBERTa/Sentence-
Transformers) trained with Triplet Loss (
, , ).
• Attribution Score: Cosine distance
computation between high-dimensional
embeddings: outputting a normalized
Attribution Confidence Score (
) between an unknown alias
and a suspected known actor.
Anchor Positive Negative
0.0 to 1.0
Dark Web Persona
Matching(Shin et al.,
2025)
Stylometry alone
produces false positives
if two actors write
casually in the same
dialect; behavioral topic
clustering is needed to
establish shared domain
intent.
• BERTopic Pipeline: Automated
extraction of core operational themes
(e.g., bulletproof hosting, specific
exploit kits, carding batches).
• Ensemble Scorer: Combined scoring
formula:
.
Final Match Confidence =
w
 
(Stylometric Similarity) +1
w  (BERTopic Semantic Overlap)2

=== PAGE 7 ===
4. Autonomous Ingestion, Pipeline Architecture & Analytical UI
Problem Statement Component:
Developing an end-to-end autonomous collection pipeline, backend data contextualization,
analyst GUI with timeline querying, and multi-format reporting (CSV, JSON, PDF).
Resource What It Reveals About the
Problem Statement
Concrete Artifacts to Extract &
Implement
Stem (Tor Controller) Scrapers cannot crawl onion
sites like clearnet sites;
circuits drop frequently,
latency is high, and DNS
lookups must be prevented
from leaking to clearnet
resolvers.
• Proxy Routing: SOCKS5
controller forcing all network calls
through
socks5h://127.0.0.1:9050
(ensuring remote DNS resolution
over Tor).
• Circuit Orchestration: Tor
control port scripts using
signal(Signal.NEWNYM) to
acquire fresh exit nodes and
circuits when scraping fails or
encounters rate limits.
• Service Health Checks: Scripts
to download hidden service
descriptors to register status (
Active vs. Offline).
Celery + Redis Heavy NLP feature extraction,
blockchain parsing, and deep
web crawling will freeze the
analyst UI if run
synchronously.
• Distributed Task Pipeline:
- Queue 1 (Ingestion): Periodic
headless crawling of
forums/markets.
- Queue 2 (Extraction): Regex
identification of PGP keys, wallets,
and onions.
- Queue 3 (ML Inference):
Stylometric feature engineering
and embedding calculation.

Cytoscape.js / React
Flow
Investigators need an
interactive, explorable canvas
to discover hidden link
clusters, filter temporal
paths, and export evidentiary
proofs.
• Frontend UI Elements: Graph
rendering engine with node color-
coding (e.g., Red = Actor, Green =
Wallet, Blue = PGP).
• Timeline Filter: Slider control
querying records between
last_scan_date and creation
dates.
• Export Handlers: Client-side
serializers mapping the graph and
actor intelligence cards into
formatted JSON schemas, CSV
tables, and structured PDF dossier
reports.

The original ShadowTrace proposal captures analyst workflows and confidence scoring well, but
it fundamentally evades two of the three core technical mandates in the problem statement.
The specific breakdown of what works, what is shaky, and what is broken or missing is detailed
below, followed by the fully integrated, upgraded solution document.
Gap Analysis: Original Document vs. Problem Statement
Positive (What Hits the Mark)
Not Sure (Shaky Implementations & Weak Spots)
Asymmetric, Explainable Confidence Scoring: The distinction between "hard"
cryptographic/infrastructure signals and "soft" linguistic/temporal signals prevents AI
hallucinations and accidental auto-attribution.
Analyst Decision Auditing & Human-in-the-Loop Workflow: The multi-tier review process
(Analyst confirmation  Senior Analyst export gate) directly satisfies forensic evidentiary
standards for threat intelligence handoffs.
→
Classical Feature Extraction: Incorporating vocabulary richness (Yule's K) and function-
word frequencies provides a solid baseline for stylistic comparison.
Exact-Match Crypto Wallet & PGP Tracking: Darknet actors rarely reuse naked Bitcoin
addresses or single PGP master keys across different markets today. Threat actors utilize
HD wallet address generation (BIP-32/44) and subkeys. Relying strictly on exact regex
matching will cause your attribution pipeline to miss more than 80% of cross-market
"xmr": ["888tNkZrPN6JsEts3Ju53DbS677Qw6Jw..."]
   }
 },
"infrastructure_indicators": {
"onion_addresses": ["examplemarket7xy...onion"],
"detected_clearnet_ip": "198.51.100.42",
"correlation_mechanism": "Apache mod_status leak + JARM hash match",
"jarm_hash": "27d27d27d00027d00042d42d42d27d..."
 },
"stylometric_signature": {
"yules_k": 82.4,
"punctuation_anomaly_score": 0.74,
"top_bertopic_clusters": ["Zero-Day Exploits", "Citadel Leaks"]

Not Sure (Shaky Implementations & Weak Spots)
Asymmetric, Explainable Confidence Scoring: The distinction between "hard"
cryptographic/infrastructure signals and "soft" linguistic/temporal signals prevents AI
hallucinations and accidental auto-attribution.
Analyst Decision Auditing & Human-in-the-Loop Workflow: The multi-tier review process
(Analyst confirmation  Senior Analyst export gate) directly satisfies forensic evidentiary
standards for threat intelligence handoffs.
→
Classical Feature Extraction: Incorporating vocabulary richness (Yule's K) and function-
word frequencies provides a solid baseline for stylistic comparison.
Exact-Match Crypto Wallet & PGP Tracking: Darknet actors rarely reuse naked Bitcoin
addresses or single PGP master keys across different markets today. Threat actors utilize
HD wallet address generation (BIP-32/44) and subkeys. Relying strictly on exact regex
matching will cause your attribution pipeline to miss more than 80% of cross-market

=== PAGE 10 ===
Negative (Critical Loopholes & Omitted Mandates)
ShadowTrace v2: Unified Dark Web De-Anonymization
Platform
End-to-End Architectural & Engineering Specification
1. Problem Statement & Operational Scope
Modern threat actors exploit Tor hidden services to run illicit operations—including zero-day
exploits, ransomware infrastructure, illicit narcotics, and money laundering—relying on layered
pseudonymity. They cycle forum handles, operate across disparate marketplaces, and mask
backend infrastructure.
ShadowTrace v2 is an end-to-end intelligence platform that automates the collection,
contextualization, graph-linkage, and deanonymization of dark web threat actors. The platform
targets three operational layers:
transactions. You must implement blockchain transaction clustering heuristics (Common-
Input Ownership).
Stylometry on Short Posts: Relying solely on bag-of-words and function-word
distributions without deep embeddings degrades severely on dark web posts under 100
words. Topic modeling is also missing: an actor's linguistic style must be coupled with what
they sell or discuss (domain intent).
Export Deficiencies: The document only implements server-side PDF generation, omitting
the problem statement's mandatory JSON and CSV forensic data exports.
Complete Evasion of Infrastructure De-anonymization: The problem statement explicitly
demands finding Tor misconfigurations (Apache mod_status, SSL cert leaks, JARM
signatures, descriptor anomalies) and linking them to clearnet origin IPs via
Censys/Shodan. ShadowTrace deliberately scoped this out under the guise of an "analyst-
only, no real-world unmasking" boundary. This will fail evaluation immediately if left
unaddressed.
Lack of Autonomous Ingestion: The problem statement requires the system to work in an
"autonomous mode, continuously gathering footprints." ShadowTrace relies strictly on
manual batch imports of pre-scraped text files and synthetic seed dumps. It lacks an
active crawler, Tor proxy orchestrator (Stem), and task queue (Celery/Redis).
Relational Database Instead of a Knowledge Graph: The problem statement mandates a
unified relationship graph of handles, keys, wallets, and trust links. ShadowTrace relegates
graph visualization and graph modeling to "Future Work," relying instead on flat
PostgreSQL tables. Flat relational models cannot handle multi-hop graph traversals.
Negative (Critical Loopholes & Omitted Mandates)
ShadowTrace v2: Unified Dark Web De-Anonymization
Platform
End-to-End Architectural & Engineering Specification
1. Problem Statement & Operational Scope
Modern threat actors exploit Tor hidden services to run illicit operations—including zero-day
exploits, ransomware infrastructure, illicit narcotics, and money laundering—relying on layered
pseudonymity. They cycle forum handles, operate across disparate marketplaces, and mask
backend infrastructure.
ShadowTrace v2 is an end-to-end intelligence platform that automates the collection,
contextualization, graph-linkage, and deanonymization of dark web threat actors. The platform
targets three operational layers:
transactions. You must implement blockchain transaction clustering heuristics (Common-
Input Ownership).
Stylometry on Short Posts: Relying solely on bag-of-words and function-word
distributions without deep embeddings degrades severely on dark web posts under 100
words. Topic modeling is also missing: an actor's linguistic style must be coupled with what
they sell or discuss (domain intent).
Export Deficiencies: The document only implements server-side PDF generation, omitting
the problem statement's mandatory JSON and CSV forensic data exports.
Complete Evasion of Infrastructure De-anonymization: The problem statement explicitly
demands finding Tor misconfigurations (Apache mod_status, SSL cert leaks, JARM
signatures, descriptor anomalies) and linking them to clearnet origin IPs via
Censys/Shodan. ShadowTrace deliberately scoped this out under the guise of an "analyst-

unaddressed.
Lack of Autonomous Ingestion: The problem statement requires the system to work in an
"autonomous mode, continuously gathering footprints." ShadowTrace relies strictly on
manual batch imports of pre-scraped text files and synthetic seed dumps. It lacks an
active crawler, Tor proxy orchestrator (Stem), and task queue (Celery/Redis).
Relational Database Instead of a Knowledge Graph: The problem statement mandates a
unified relationship graph of handles, keys, wallets, and trust links. ShadowTrace relegates
graph visualization and graph modeling to "Future Work," relying instead on flat
PostgreSQL tables. Flat relational models cannot handle multi-hop graph traversals.

2. End-to-End System Workflow
Infrastructure Unmasking: Identifying misconfigurations and cryptographic fingerprints
in Tor services to match hidden backends to clearnet IP addresses via Internet-wide scan
telemetry.
1.
Multi-Marketplace Entity Graphing: Ingesting cryptographic keys, transaction graphs,
and forum metadata into a high-performance property graph to trace multi-hop entity
relationships.
2.
AI Identity Attribution: Combining deep stylometric neural embeddings with topic
modeling to link rebranded or migrated personas to known threat actors across forums
and marketplaces.
3.

                ▼

                │
                ▼
  [ Celery Distributed Message Broker (Redis) ]
    ├──  Queue 1: Infrastructure Scraper (OnionScan + HTTP Fingerprinting)
    ├──  Queue 2: Crypto & Key Extractor (RFC 4880 PGP + Blockchain Heuristics)
    └──  Queue 3: AI Stylometry Engine (Writeprints + Siamese Transformer)
                │
                ▼
[ Correlation & Identity Resolution Engine (Blended Scoring) ]
                │
                ▼
[ Storage Layer: Neo4j (Graph) + PostgreSQL (Audit/Profiles) + MinIO (Raw) ]
                │
                ▼
[ Analyst Intelligence Dashboard (React Flow / Cytoscape.js) ]
    ├──  Misconfiguration Radar & Clearnet Match Visualizer
    ├──  Multi-Hop Identity Graph & Timeline Query Engine
    └──  Multi-Format Forensic Exporter (JSON / CSV / PDF)
Ingestion & Discovery: Autonomous daemons scrape registered .onion forums,
markets, and paste dumps on schedule.
1.
Deep Fingerprinting: As web content arrives, infrastructure scanners probe hidden
endpoints for status pages and certificates, cryptographic modules extract master PGP
fingerprints, and NLP workers parse message content.
2.
Graph Ingestion: Entities (Handles, Keys, Wallets, Servers, Domains) are mapped into
Neo4j as nodes, while co-occurrences, transactions, and PGP signatures become directed

3. Deep Technical Modules
3.1 Infrastructure De-anonymization & Clearnet Matching
Addressed: Misconfiguration discovery, origin server identification, SSL/banner correlation.
3.2 Knowledge Graph & Cryptographic Entity Resolution
Addressed: Cross-marketplace entity mapping, PGP matching, cryptocurrency transaction
tracking.
edges.
AI Persona Correlation: An unknown or newly emerged handle is vector-embedded and
cross-referenced against historical threat actor profiles using contrastive stylometric
networks and BERTopic semantic distributions.
4.
Analyst Review & Verification: The intelligence dashboard flags candidate links with an
explainability breakdown. Analysts confirm or reject associations, dynamically merging
nodes into unified actor profiles.
5.
Dissemination: Investigators filter intelligence across temporal timelines and export
court-defensible dossiers in CSV, JSON, and PDF formats.
6.
OnionScan Engine: Automates active scans against target .onion sites via local Tor
SOCKS5 proxies (socks5h://127.0.0.1:9050). It probes for open /server-status
and /server-info endpoints to extract Apache server client tables that reveal backend
clearnet IPs. It also checks for directory traversal exposures (.git/HEAD, .env,
configuration backups).
Cryptographic & Asset Fingerprinting:
SSL/TLS Leaf Extraction: Scrapes SSL certificates served over Tor; computes their
SHA-256 digests and extracts Common Names (CN) and Subject Alternative Names
(SAN) that unintentionally reference clearnet FQDNs.
JARM Signatures: Sends 10 tailored TLS Client Hello packets to the hidden service,
generating a 62-character cryptographic fingerprint of the underlying TLS stack.
Favicon Hashes: Computes MurmurHash3 values on static application icons (
favicon.ico).
Clearnet Correlation Engine: Dispatches automated API queries to Censys and Shodan
using the extracted fingerprints:
Matching clearnet nodes identify candidate physical hosting providers and public IP
addresses.
Query: services.tls.certificates.leaf_data.fingerprint: [SHA-256]

=== PAGE 13 ===
3.3 AI-Based Stylometry & Behavioral Profiling
Addressed: Authorship verification, migrated alias attribution, semantic profiling.
PGP Identity Parsing (RFC 4880): Raw PGP ASCII blocks are parsed to extract the primary
160-bit SHA-1 / 256-bit SHA-2 Key Fingerprints, subkey bindings, and user ID strings (
Name <email@domain>). The system rejects easily spoofed 8-byte short IDs, anchoring
nodes strictly on cryptographic fingerprints.
Blockchain Transaction Clustering: Rather than relying exclusively on exact address
matches, transaction outputs are ingested via RPC into an automated heuristic pipeline:
Common-Input Ownership Heuristic: When multiple cryptocurrency addresses are
spent as inputs within a single transaction, the engine applies union-find logic to
cluster those addresses under the same threat-actor entity.
Change Address Identification: Evaluates round-value outputs and output address
reuse to distinguish vendor change addresses from recipient payouts.
Neo4j Graph Implementation:
Nodes:(:ThreatActor), (:ForumHandle), (:PGPKey), (:CryptoWallet), 
(:ServerInfrastructure).
Edges:[:POSTED_AS], [:USES_KEY], [:CONTROLS_WALLET], 
[:ASSOCIATED_IP], [:TRANSFERRED_TO].
Automated Cluster Ingestion: When two handles share an identical master PGP
fingerprint or belong to the same input-spend wallet cluster, a deterministic Cypher
transaction merges the associated ForumHandle nodes under an overarching 
(:ThreatActor) cluster.
Writeprints Feature Extraction: Converts unstructured forum posts into an orthogonal
feature vector:
Lexical & Syntactic: Yule’s characteristic , Simpson’s index , Hapax Legomena
frequency, average syllables per word, sentence-length distribution variances.
K D
Orthographic: Punctuation anomaly rates, capitalization entropy, and
markdown/bullet formatting structures.
Siamese Neural Network (Authorship Verification): A Transformer backbone (fine-tuned
RoBERTa-large) maps token sequences into a 1024-dimensional metric space. The network
is optimized via Triplet Loss:
Where  is an anchor post from a known actor,  is a post from a suspect new alias, and 
 is a negative sample from an unrelated author. The Euclidean distance between
L(A,P ,N) = max ∥f(A) −f(P )∥ − ∥f(A) −f(N)∥ +α, 0( 2 2 )
A P

=== PAGE 14 ===
3.4 Autonomous Pipeline Architecture
Addressed: Headless continuous gathering, task distribution, queue management.
4. Blended Confidence Scoring Engine
Attribution links are evaluated using an asymmetric heuristic rather than a naive linear average.
embeddings generates a normalized stylometric similarity score.
BERTopic Semantic Modeling: Extracts latent operational topics across candidate
handles (e.g., specific malware families, initial access brokerage, cashout mechanics).
Authorship attribution requires both a high stylometric similarity and an overlapping
semantic topic distribution, minimizing false positives from common dialects.
Tor Controller (Stem): Manages local Tor instances, dynamically cycling circuits using 
signal(Signal.NEWNYM) upon encountering rate limits, and monitoring hidden service
descriptor health.
Asynchronous Task Queue (Celery + Redis):
Crawl tasks run independently of UI operations.
Worker processes partition data: lightweight extractors run concurrently with GPU-
bound AI stylometry tasks.
Storage Tiering:
PostgreSQL: Normalized persistence for user accounts, role-based access controls,
immutable investigation audit logs, and system metrics.
Neo4j: Property graph engine for all relationship and path-traversal queries.
MinIO (S3-Compatible Object Store): Immutable storage for raw scraped HTML,
server banners, and generated evidence exports.

Shared Master PGP
Fingerprint, Common-
Input Wallet Cluster

6. Implementation Deliverables & Evaluation Checklist
Misconfiguration Inspector: Displays live-rendered HTTP header diffs, SSL certificate
chains, and side-by-side Censys/Shodan infrastructure comparisons.
Forensic Export Engine: Compiles evidentiary trails into three distinct outputs:
CSV: Tabular dumps of all extracted indicators (IOCs: IPs, wallets, PGP fingerprints)
for ingestion into SIEM/SOAR platforms.
JSON: Fully structured threat intelligence schemas (STIX 2.1 compliant) detailing
persona mappings and confidence scores.
Report Format (PDF): Formal executive dossiers complete with chain-of-custody
metadata, analyst verification signatures, and graphical link diagrams.

socks5h with dynamic circuit rotation.

generator with Censys/Shodan API integration.

traversal operations.
[x] AI Attribution: PyTorch/Hugging Face pipeline computing stylometric metric vectors
and BERTopic clustering on ingested forum dumps.

Seat 2: Cryptographic & Financial Intelligence Specialist

Negative
Seat 3: AI, Stylometry & NLP Specialist
Tor v3 Descriptor Parsing: Tor v3 hidden service descriptors encrypt their introduction
points and metadata. Extracting clock-skew and descriptor anomalies from raw v3
descriptors in an automated pipeline requires specialized directory authority telemetry
that is technically brittle.
Anti-DDoS WAF Bypass: Modern darknet marketplaces protect endpoints behind services
like EndGame, rotating Proof-of-Work (PoW) captchas, or DDoS-Guard. Simple automated 
requests/Stem calls will routinely get blocked at the gate without headless browser
automation (e.g., undetected-playwright).
No Handling of Upstream CDN / Reverse Proxies: Matching an SSL certificate or JARM
hash to a clearnet IP often leads directly to Cloudflare, Fastly, or a bulletproof reverse
proxy—not the real physical origin backend. The solution lacks a hop to resolve reverse-
proxy IPs to true backend origin servers.
RFC 4880 Master Fingerprint Matching: Prioritizing 160-bit SHA-1 / 256-bit SHA-2
fingerprints over 8-byte short IDs prevents spoofing and key collision vulnerabilities.
DOCX
Deterministic Transaction Graphing: Ingesting Bitcoin inputs with the Common-Input
Ownership Heuristic bridges fragmented wallet addresses into a unified cluster, resolving
pseudonymous multi-accounting.
Graph Schema Alignment: The Neo4j data model (Actor Key Wallet) directly
satisfies the problem statement's requirement to map multi-marketplace trust links.
→ →
Privacy Coin (Monero / XMR) Reality: Over 70% of modern dark web transactions use
Monero. Because Monero utilizes Ring Confidential Transactions (RingCT) and stealth
addresses, blockchain clustering heuristics cannot group XMR transactions. The solution
only exact-matches raw Monero public addresses, which are rarely reused across markets.
CoinJoin / Mixer Blindspot: The heuristics assume clean, direct blockchain transactions. If
a threat actor routes funds through Wasabi, Whirlpool, or cross-chain bridges, the
Common-Input heuristic breaks completely and can cause massive false-positive entity
mergers if not guarded by taint-threshold filters.

Seat 3: AI, Stylometry & NLP Specialist
Tor v3 Descriptor Parsing: Tor v3 hidden service descriptors encrypt their introduction
points and metadata. Extracting clock-skew and descriptor anomalies from raw v3
descriptors in an automated pipeline requires specialized directory authority telemetry
that is technically brittle.
Anti-DDoS WAF Bypass: Modern darknet marketplaces protect endpoints behind services
like EndGame, rotating Proof-of-Work (PoW) captchas, or DDoS-Guard. Simple automated 
requests/Stem calls will routinely get blocked at the gate without headless browser
automation (e.g., undetected-playwright).
No Handling of Upstream CDN / Reverse Proxies: Matching an SSL certificate or JARM
hash to a clearnet IP often leads directly to Cloudflare, Fastly, or a bulletproof reverse
proxy—not the real physical origin backend. The solution lacks a hop to resolve reverse-
proxy IPs to true backend origin servers.

Council Summary: Remaining Loopholes & Fixes
Live Network Fragility: Tor hidden services are notoriously unstable, with high latency and
regular outages. Relying exclusively on live .onion crawls during an evaluation or demo
will cause timeouts. You must implement a pre-indexed seed database with an offline
replay mode to guarantee system stability if live endpoints drop.

2. CoinJoin / Mixer Blindspots & Monero (XMR) Privacy Evasion (Severity:
Medium)
3. Adversarial Stylometry & LLM Sanitization Evasion (Severity: Medium)
Dynamic PoW Solver & Circuit Pool: Implement worker-level circuit pools that monitor
HTTP response codes. If a circuit encounters an HTTP 429, 503, or CAPTCHA challenge,
Stem automatically triggers Signal.NEWNYM, establishes a fresh Tor circuit across clean
guard/exit relays, and dispatches CPU workers to compute PoW hashcash challenges
locally.
Deterministic Dual-Mode Fallback: To prevent demo or evaluation failure during dark web
outages, deploy a Hybrid Ingestion Controller. The ingestion controller operates in Live
Probe Mode by default, but automatically falls back to an immutable, pre-indexed 
Snapshot Replay Cache (stored in MinIO) if a target .onion is unresponsive for more
than 45 seconds, ensuring seamless evaluation uptime.
CoinJoin / Mixer Taint Pruning: The standard Common-Input Ownership heuristic fails
when threat actors pass funds through Wasabi, JoinMarket, or Whirlpool mixers.
Implement an automated Mixer Topology Detector:
If a Bitcoin transaction displays  inputs and  equal-value outputs (e.g., exact 
 BTC or  BTC denominations), the pipeline tags it as IS_COINJOIN.
≥ 5 ≥ 5
0.05 0.1
The graph engine halts automatic clustering on IS_COINJOIN transactions,
preventing false-positive mergers across unrelated entities and routing the
transaction to an unclustered MixerHop edge.
Monero Dual-Track Intelligence: Acknowledge cryptographic constraints (RingCT, stealth
addresses) directly:
Off-Chain Behavioral Correlation: Track Monero payment patterns across forums and
markets by extracting public vendor addresses, transaction memo structures, and
advertised PGP payment confirmations.
Temporal Volume Matching: Flag transactions where market listing creation
timestamps closely correlate ( ) with public ledger block issuance times and
known deposit-sweep addresses.
< Δt
Database Representation: Classify Bitcoin entities as HEURISTIC_CLUSTERED and
Monero entities as DETERMINISTIC_EXACT_MATCH to maintain cryptographic
accuracy.
Adversarial AI Sanitization Filter: Modern threat actors use local LLMs (e.g., Llama) or
multi-hop machine translation (Russian  German  English) to strip stylistic quirks.

token log-rank analysis):

4. CDN & Reverse-Proxy Masking (Severity: Low/Medium)
Part 2: Production System Architecture & Technical Specifications
Module A: Autonomous Ingestion & Resilient Crawling Engine
If a text block exhibits unnaturally low perplexity and high stylistic uniformity, the
system flags the text as ADVERSARIALLY_SANITIZED.
The pipeline automatically downweights the stylometric score to near zero (
) to prevent false attribution.
w  →stylometry 0
Shift to Behavioral & Lexical Artifacts: When stylometry is suppressed, the attribution
engine relies on unaffected operational signals:
BERTopic Domain Intent: Core technical topics (e.g., specific ransomware builder
nomenclature, zero-day CVE references).
Operational Tempo: Temporal diurnal activity histograms (posting hours relative to
UTC timezones).
Technical Artifacts: PGP key metadata, embedded crypto addresses, and unique
software syntax.
Autonomous ASN Classification: When Censys or Shodan queries match a JARM, SSL
certificate, or favicon hash to a public clearnet IP, pass the IP through an automated BGP
routing table check:
If the resolved IP belongs to a known CDN / DDoS mitigation Autonomous System
(e.g., Cloudflare ASN 13335, Fastly ASN 54113, Akamai ASN 20940, DDoS-Guard ASN
57724), tag the node in Neo4j as (:EdgeProxy) rather than (:OriginServer).
Direct Origin Verification Heuristics:
Historical DNS & SSL SAN Mining: Query historical SSL certificate databases (Censys
Historical / crt.sh) to identify subject alternative names (e.g., 
origin.backend.target.com, direct.target.com) configured before the
service moved behind the CDN.
SNI & Direct Host-Header Probing: Send direct HTTP requests to candidate non-CDN
IP ranges discovered within the same hosting subnet, setting the HTTP Host header
to the target onion service's clearnet domain to confirm origin response parity.
Technology Stack: Python, Stem, Playwright Stealth, Redis, Celery.
Execution Flow:
Celery scheduler dispatches distributed crawl jobs across a pool of isolated Tor
daemon containers.

// Deterministic Master Identity Merge on Shared PGP Key
MATCH (h1:ForumHandle)-[:SIGNS_WITH]->(k:PGPKey)<-[:SIGNS_WITH]-(h2:ForumHandle)
WHERE h1 <> h2
MERGE (ta:ThreatActor {cluster_id: k.fingerprint})

MERGE (h2)-[:OPERATED_BY]->(ta)
RETURN ta, h1, h2;

The Production Tech Stack & Justifications
Component Technology Selection Justification
Tor Controller Python Stem Provides granular, low-level control over Tor daemon circuits
. Enables dynamic IP switching (NEWNYM) to bypass circuit
rate limits and prevents clearnet DNS leaks via socks5h.
Crawling
Engine
Playwright
Stealth /
Camoufox
Plain HTTP libraries (requests, urllib) fail
immediately against darknet anti-DDoS gates (EndGame,
rotating PoW challenges). Headless stealth browsers
execute challenge scripts and render dynamic JavaScript.
Autonomous Ingestion & Crawling: A Python Stem daemon continuously cycles Tor
circuits via SOCKS5 (socks5h://127.0.0.1:9050). Headless stealth browser workers
bypass Proof-of-Work (PoW) captchas to scrape marketplace listings, forum threads, and
paste dumps. If a target is offline, an automated fallback serves cached snapshots from
MinIO object storage.
1.
DOCX+ 2
Asynchronous Indicator Extraction: Scraped raw content is queued in Redis and picked
up by distributed Celery workers. The jobs run across three parallel pipelines:
2.
DOCX
Infrastructure Scraper: Probes for exposed status endpoints, computes JARM and
favicon hashes, and queries Censys/Shodan for clearnet IP matches.
Cryptographic & Financial Extractor: Parses RFC 4880 PGP blocks and clusters
Bitcoin UTXO transaction inputs.
 DOCX
AI NLP Worker: Strips quoting artifacts, checks for LLM sanitization, extracts
stylometric features, and generates contextual vector embeddings.
 DOCX
Graph Mapping & Entity Resolution: Extracted artifacts are ingested into Neo4j. Co-
occurring handles, cryptographic keys, wallet clusters, and infrastructure indicators
populate graph nodes and directed edges. Deterministic link triggers automatically cluster
handles that share hard cryptographic proof.
3.
DOCX+ 1
Scoring & Attribution: The scoring engine computes an explainable confidence score (
to ). Hard cryptographic and infrastructure matches override soft signals, while purely
stylometric and semantic matches are capped to prevent false-positive auto-merges.
4. 0.0
1.0
DOCX+ 1
Analyst Review & Verification: Analysts inspect side-by-side signal breakdowns and
confidence scores on the dashboard. Confirmed links update the persona cluster in Neo4j;
rejected links are permanently recorded in an immutable PostgreSQL audit trail.
5.
DOCX+ 1
Dissemination: Investigators filter intelligence by timeline or confidence tier and export
results in CSV, STIX 2.1 JSON, or PDF formats.
6.

=== PAGE 31 ===
Component Technology Selection Justification
Infrastructure
Recon
OnionScan Core
+ Python ssl
Automates direct targeting of common misconfiguration
vectors (mod_status, .git/HEAD, exposed
environment variables). Extracts TLS leaf certificates,
generates MurmurHash3 favicon values, and creates 62-
character JARM hashes.
Internet-Wide
Scanners
Censys API &
Shodan API
Maintains pre-indexed internet databases to match JARM
hashes, favicon fingerprints, and TLS certificate serials
against clearnet web servers without conducting
unauthorized internet port scans.
Task Queue &
Broker
Celery + Redis Decouples resource-intensive transformer inference and
darknet scraping from the API. Manages task concurrency
and ensures crawler failures do not block analyst requests.
Knowledge
Graph DB
Neo4j Standard relational databases fail at multi-hop entity
queries. Neo4j handles high-depth traversals across
handles, wallets, PGP keys, and servers via optimized
Cypher queries.
Relational DB PostgreSQL Stores user access control (RBAC), investigation case notes,
review queues, and immutable forensic audit logs.
Object Storage MinIO (S3-
compatible)
Persists compressed raw HTML, raw forum dumps, and
compiled PDF case dossiers with SHA-256 integrity
verification.
AI / NLP Models RoBERTa-large
(Siamese) +
BERTopic
Fine-tuned Siamese networks map short texts into metric
embedding spaces via Triplet Loss. BERTopic extracts
operational intent, ensuring linguistic style is evaluated
alongside topic domain overlap.
Perplexity Gate Fast-DetectGPT /
Token-Log-Rank
Inspects text entropy to identify machine-translated or
LLM-sanitized writing styles, preventing false stylometric
attributions.
Frontend
Framework
React + React
Flow /
Cytoscape.js
Provides an interactive canvas for visualizing graph
relationships, expanding connected nodes, and scrubbing
across temporal timelines.
Dossier
Exporter
WeasyPrint /
Puppeteer
Generates exportable, court-defensible PDF dossiers
alongside standard CSV and STIX 2.1 JSON data formats.
Technical Deep-Dive Across the Core Pillars
1. Infrastructure De-anonymization & Clearnet Correlation
DOCX
DOCX+ 1
DOCX
DOCX
DOCX
DOCX
Misconfiguration Probing: Scans endpoints for Apache mod_status to extract internal
client IP tables and server uptime counters. Evaluates .git/HEAD or .env paths to

=== PAGE 32 ===
2. Multi-Marketplace Entity Linkage & Graph Resolution
3. AI Stylometry & Behavioral Profiling
4. Hardened Blended Confidence Scoring Engine
Attribution uses an asymmetric scoring formula where deterministic indicators override
probabilistic estimates:
locate leaked credentials or deployment metadata.
Cryptographic Signatures: Extracts full SSL leaf certificate chains to identify clearnet
Subject Alternative Names (SANs). Employs JARM active scanning (10 custom TLS Client
Hello packets) to map backend operating system and OpenSSL configurations.
Origin vs. Edge Proxy Resolution: Clearnet IPs discovered via Censys/Shodan are cross-
referenced with BGP routing tables. Matches returning CDN ASNs (Cloudflare, Fastly,
Akamai) are tagged as (:EdgeProxy). Non-CDN hosting providers are validated via
direct SNI and HTTP Host header replay to confirm origin identity.
RFC 4880 PGP Parsing: Programmatically extracts the full 160-bit SHA-1 or 256-bit SHA-2
Master Key Fingerprint from ASCII-armored blocks, discarding easily spoofed 8-byte short
IDs.
 DOCX
UTXO Transaction Graphing: Applies the Common-Input Ownership Heuristic to combine
multiple inputs within a single Bitcoin transaction into an entity cluster.
 DOCX
Mixer Taint Detection: Analyzes transactions for CoinJoin characteristics (  inputs, 
 identical outputs). If detected, automated clustering is suspended, and the transaction
is marked as an unclustered MixerHop to protect against false graph mergers.
≥ 5 ≥
5
Monero Tracking: Monero is tracked through direct public address matches and off-chain
correlation, pairing forum escrow deposit timestamps with listing activity.
Writeprints Extraction: Ingests forum posts (enforcing a 500-word minimum baseline)
and extracts over 400 features, including vocabulary richness (Yule’s K, Simpson’s D),
punctuation distribution, and function-word frequency vectors.
 DOCX
Siamese Embedding: Token sequences are fed into a fine-tuned Siamese RoBERTa model
to output 1024-dimensional metric vectors. Distances are scored via cosine similarity.
Adversarial LLM Filter: Prior to stylometric scoring, posts are screened by an adversarial
perplexity filter. If the writing displays low entropy typical of LLM rewriting, the stylometry
weight is suppressed to zero ( ), and attribution falls back strictly on
technical indicators.
w  →stylometry 0
DOCX

Cryptographic Signatures: Extracts full SSL leaf certificate chains to identify clearnet
Subject Alternative Names (SANs). Employs JARM active scanning (10 custom TLS Client
Hello packets) to map backend operating system and OpenSSL configurations.
Origin vs. Edge Proxy Resolution: Clearnet IPs discovered via Censys/Shodan are cross-
referenced with BGP routing tables. Matches returning CDN ASNs (Cloudflare, Fastly,
Akamai) are tagged as (:EdgeProxy). Non-CDN hosting providers are validated via
direct SNI and HTTP Host header replay to confirm origin identity.
RFC 4880 PGP Parsing: Programmatically extracts the full 160-bit SHA-1 or 256-bit SHA-2
Master Key Fingerprint from ASCII-armored blocks, discarding easily spoofed 8-byte short
IDs.
 DOCX
UTXO Transaction Graphing: Applies the Common-Input Ownership Heuristic to combine
multiple inputs within a single Bitcoin transaction into an entity cluster.
 DOCX
Mixer Taint Detection: Analyzes transactions for CoinJoin characteristics (  inputs, 
 identical outputs). If detected, automated clustering is suspended, and the transaction
is marked as an unclustered MixerHop to protect against false graph mergers.

5
Monero Tracking: Monero is tracked through direct public address matches and off-chain
correlation, pairing forum escrow deposit timestamps with listing activity.
Writeprints Extraction: Ingests forum posts (enforcing a 500-word minimum baseline)
and extracts over 400 features, including vocabulary richness (Yule’s K, Simpson’s D),
punctuation distribution, and function-word frequency vectors.
 DOCX
Siamese Embedding: Token sequences are fed into a fine-tuned Siamese RoBERTa model
to output 1024-dimensional metric vectors. Distances are scored via cosine similarity.
Adversarial LLM Filter: Prior to stylometric scoring, posts are screened by an adversarial
perplexity filter. If the writing displays low entropy typical of LLM rewriting, the stylometry
weight is suppressed to zero ( ), and attribution falls back strictly on
technical indicators.

2. Monero (XMR) Privacy Guarantees (Fundamental Limit)

The Bottleneck: The problem statement highlights detecting "descriptor inconsistencies"
to deanonymize services. Unlike v2, modern Tor v3 onion service descriptors encrypt their
introduction points and metadata using a key derived from the .onion address itself.
Reality Check: An external observer cannot passively extract server metadata or timing
leaks from a v3 descriptor without active attacks on HSDir (Hidden Service Directory)
relays or controlling significant Tor network consensus weight. Probing web application
misconfigurations is practical; parsing v3 descriptor anomalies autonomously from the
outside is largely ineffective.
The Bottleneck: The system uses Bitcoin transaction graphs (UTXO common-input
ownership) for cross-market tracking.
 DOCX
Reality Check: The darknet economy has largely transitioned to Monero. Because Monero
employs RingCT, Ring Signatures, and Stealth Addresses, mathematical wallet clustering
across transactions is impossible on the base layer. If a threat actor avoids reusing static
Monero deposit addresses in public forum posts, blockchain analysis yields zero linkage.
The tool cannot reliably track actors who transact exclusively in Monero.

employs RingCT, Ring Signatures, and Stealth Addresses, mathematical wallet clustering
across transactions is impossible on the base layer. If a threat actor avoids reusing static
Monero deposit addresses in public forum posts, blockchain analysis yields zero linkage.
The tool cannot reliably track actors who transact exclusively in Monero.

4. Adversarial Stylometry & Native Dialect Drift (ML False Positives)
5. Legal Boundaries of Active Origin Probing (Compliance Risk)
The Bottleneck: The system relies on autonomous crawlers running across headless
browsers.
Reality Check: Major darknet forums run custom, dynamic Proof-of-Work systems (such as
EndGame or 60-second client-side WASM computations) specifically engineered to
exhaust bot resources. While Playwright handles basic JavaScript, sustaining persistent,
high-throughput autonomous scraping against aggressive anti-bot defenses over high-
latency Tor circuits frequently triggers connection drops, session timeouts, and IP bans.
The Bottleneck: Stylometric persona linking relies on stable linguistic patterns.
 DOCX
Reality Check: Threat actors who run their communications through simple translation
pipelines (e.g., Russian  German  English) completely eliminate function-word
patterns, sentence distributions, and orthographic quirks. While an adversarial AI detector
flags LLM output, it cannot reliably detect standard machine translation. In those
instances, soft stylometric signals can produce misleading similarity scores among non-
native English speakers.
→ →
The Bottleneck: The system actively issues HTTP requests to /server-status, 
.git/HEAD, and candidate backend subnets to confirm clearnet origin IPs.
Reality Check: In many jurisdictions, sending unauthorized payloads to discovered clearnet
IP addresses to force status disclosures or verify backend hosting falls under active
network scanning and unauthorized system access (e.g., CFAA violations or Section 43/66
of the IT Act). Without explicit statutory authority or a judicial warrant, automated active
probing must be restricted strictly to passive search engine querying (Censys/Shodan
APIs) to remain legally defensible.

ShadowTrace v2 is a hardened, operationally resilient platform engineered to deanonymize dark
web threat actors by correlating application-layer misconfigurations, multi-marketplace
cryptographic identities, and translation-resistant behavioral semantics into an explainable
property graph. The architecture accounts for operational constraints—such as Tor v3
descriptor encryption, Monero privacy protocols, dynamic anti-DDoS Proof-of-Work (PoW)
defenses, and jurisdictional scanning boundaries.

3. Hardened Module Breakdown & Evasion Mitigations
A. Application-Layer Infrastructure Correlation & Legal Boundaries
B. Multi-Marketplace Entity Linkage & Financial Realities
The Vulnerability Countered: Tor v3 hidden service descriptors encrypt metadata and
introduction points, preventing passive directory snooping. Furthermore, active network
scanning of discovered clearnet IPs can violate cybercrime statutes (e.g., CFAA, IT Act
Section 43/66).
Application-Layer Probing: Rather than attempting descriptor cryptanalysis, the engine
focuses entirely on the application layer:
Probes for Apache mod_status and mod_info exposures, reading client
connection tables that reveal true internal backend IP addresses.
 DOCX
Scrapes /git/HEAD, .env, and application error backtraces for hardcoded
clearnet endpoints, database strings, and internal staging URLs.
Computes 62-character JARM TLS client-hello hashes and MurmurHash3 favicon
values directly from the web response.
Dual-Mode Legal Operational Gate:
Passive Mode (Default): Discovered JARM hashes, SSL certificate serials, and
favicons are cross-referenced exclusively against pre-indexed internet databases
(Censys and Shodan). No raw packets are transmitted to candidate clearnet hosts.
Active Probing Mode (Warrant-Gated): Direct origin verification (such as custom SNI
and Host header probing to candidate backend subnets) is cryptographically
locked behind role-based access controls. It requires an active case identifier and a
senior authority authorization flag in PostgreSQL before execution.
 DOCX+ 1
CDN vs. Origin ASN Disambiguation: Discovered clearnet IPs are resolved against BGP
routing tables. Addresses mapped to CDN networks (Cloudflare, Akamai, Fastly) are
classified as (:EdgeProxy) nodes. Non-CDN IPs with matching cryptographic signatures
are flagged as candidate (:OriginServer) backends.
The Vulnerability Countered: Threat actors avoid single PGP key reuse and transact
heavily in privacy-preserving cryptocurrencies like Monero (XMR), breaking simple wallet-
regex matching and Bitcoin heuristics.
RFC 4880 Cryptographic Anchoring: Parses raw ASCII PGP blocks to extract unalterable
160-bit SHA-1 or 256-bit SHA-2 Master Key Fingerprints, user IDs (Name <email@domain>
), and creation timestamps. Truncated 8-byte short IDs are rejected to prevent key-
collision spoofing.

C. Translation-Resilient AI Profiling & Behavioral Analysis
Implements the Common-Input Ownership heuristic using union-find logic, merging
distinct input addresses spent within a single transaction into a unified entity cluster.
DOCX
Mixer Taint Filter: Evaluates incoming transactions for CoinJoin characteristics (
inputs,  equal-denomination outputs). When detected, automated clustering is
halted, and the transaction is mapped to an isolated (:MixerHop) edge to prevent
false-positive entity mergers.

≥ 5
Monero Dual-Track Intelligence: Avoids false claims of mathematical on-chain XMR
clustering. Instead, it tracks Monero via off-chain forensic correlation:
Ingests static public vendor addresses, subaddresses, and recurring payment IDs
published in forum listings.
Correlates vendor deposit requirements with public ledger block confirmation
timestamps ( ) and transaction volume ranges.
< Δt
In the graph database, Bitcoin addresses are tagged as HEURISTIC_CLUSTER,
whereas Monero data is classified strictly as DETERMINISTIC_EXACT_MATCH or 
TEMPORAL_CORRELATION.
// Cypher: Cross-Market Identity Resolution via Cryptographic Master Anchor
MATCH (h1:ForumHandle)-[:SIGNS_WITH]->(k:PGPKey)<-[:SIGNS_WITH]-(h2:ForumHandle)
WHERE h1 <> h2 AND NOT (h1)-[:ALIASED_WITH]-(h2)
MERGE (ta:ThreatActor {master_id: k.fingerprint})
MERGE (h1)-[:OPERATED_BY]->(ta)
MERGE (h2)-[:OPERATED_BY]->(ta)
SET ta.last_correlation_date = datetime()
RETURN ta.master_id, count(DISTINCT h1) AS LinkedHandles;
The Vulnerability Countered: Traditional stylometry (Writeprints, punctuation counters,
function-word distributions) fails when threat actors use machine translation (e.g., Russian 
 English) or local LLMs to rewrite posts.
→
Adversarial AI Sanitization Gate: Forum posts are routed through an entropy and
perplexity evaluation layer (Fast-DetectGPT). If text exhibits uniform log-rank
characteristics typical of generative LLM sanitization, the system flags the content as 
ADVERSARIALLY_SANITIZED and suspends lexical stylometric scoring.

TEMPORAL_CORRELATION.
// Cypher: Cross-Market Identity Resolution via Cryptographic Master Anchor
MATCH (h1:ForumHandle)-[:SIGNS_WITH]->(k:PGPKey)<-[:SIGNS_WITH]-(h2:ForumHandle)
WHERE h1 <> h2 AND NOT (h1)-[:ALIASED_WITH]-(h2)
MERGE (ta:ThreatActor {master_id: k.fingerprint})
MERGE (h1)-[:OPERATED_BY]->(ta)
MERGE (h2)-[:OPERATED_BY]->(ta)
SET ta.last_correlation_date = datetime()
RETURN ta.master_id, count(DISTINCT h1) AS LinkedHandles;
The Vulnerability Countered: Traditional stylometry (Writeprints, punctuation counters,
function-word distributions) fails when threat actors use machine translation (e.g., Russian 
 English) or local LLMs to rewrite posts.
→
Adversarial AI Sanitization Gate: Forum posts are routed through an entropy and
perplexity evaluation layer (Fast-DetectGPT). If text exhibits uniform log-rank
characteristics typical of generative LLM sanitization, the system flags the content as 
ADVERSARIALLY_SANITIZED and suspends lexical stylometric scoring.
Cypher  
=== PAGE 39 ===
D. Anti-DDoS, CAPTCHA & Scraper Uptime Architecture
4. Blended Asymmetric Confidence Scoring Engine
Attribution uses an asymmetric scoring formula where deterministic indicators override
probabilistic estimates:
Cross-Lingual Semantic Embeddings: When stylometric counts fail, the pipeline relies on
multilingual transformer models (LaBSE / XLM-RoBERTa). These models map sentence
semantics into an invariant vector space regardless of the operational language or
translation layer.
Operational Intent Profiling (BERTopic): Analyzes specialized technical jargon, CVE
identifiers, targeted zero-day exploits, infrastructure demands, and escrow settlement
terms. Identity matching requires both semantic vector similarity and overlapping
operational topics, neutralizing false positives caused by common forum slang.
Temporal Diurnal Analysis: Ingests posting timestamps normalized to UTC, building
activity probability distributions that identify timezone bounds (such as active operating
windows between 18:00 and 03:00 UTC) as a secondary corroborating signal.
 DOCX
The Vulnerability Countered: Dynamic Proof-of-Work (PoW) scripts, WASM challenges,
and high Tor circuit latency cause scrapers to time out and drop connections.
Headless Stealth Cluster: Scrapers run within containerized Playwright Stealth instances,
capable of executing client-side JavaScript and completing computational PoW
challenges natively.
Session Cookie Harvesting Vault: When an analyst or an automated worker successfully
passes a challenge on a major marketplace, the authenticated session state (session
cookies, CSRF tokens) is encrypted and saved into a centralized Redis Session Vault.
Secondary Celery scrapers reuse these authenticated tokens, bypassing repetitive front-
door challenge pages.
Deterministic Snapshot Replay Cache: Scraped pages and headers are mirrored into
MinIO object storage. If a target .onion service goes offline during an active analysis or
evaluation, the ingestion controller switches seamlessly to the cached snapshot without
interrupting downstream graph analytics or UI workflows.
DOCX

The end-to-end operational lifecycle translates raw dark web footprint gathering into court-
defensible threat actor deanonymization through six distinct phases.
Phase 1: Target Registration & Autonomous Ingestion Initiation
The investigation begins when an analyst defines the scope of an investigation or when the
autonomous daemon picks up scheduled collection tasks. DOCX
 
[Analyst / Admin] ──  Input Seed / Onions ── ►  [Frontend Source Modal] ── ►  [Stem & Play
Analyst Action (User Journey): The analyst logs into the dashboard via role-based access
control (RBAC). They navigate to Source & Ingestion Management and register a target 
.onion forum, market URL, or uploaded batch dump (JSON/HTML paste files). The
analyst tags the target case with an identifier (e.g., CASE-2026-X01) and sets collection
parameters (depth, crawl interval).
 DOCX+ 1
Frontend UI State:
Target Management Console displays live crawl statuses: Queued, Crawling, 
Rate-Limited, or Offline-Snapshot Active.
Real-time metrics counters display Tor circuits established, total pages fetched, and
CAPTCHAs solved.
An interactive terminal drawer shows real-time scraper log streams without blocking
the primary dashboard view.
Backend Execution:
The API dispatches crawl events to the Celery task broker backed by Redis.
 DOCX
Stem controller spawns containerized Tor instances and configures SOCKS5 proxy
routing (socks5h://127.0.0.1:9050).

JavaScript/WASM Proof-of-Work (PoW) challenges, extract authenticated session
cookies, and save them into the centralized Redis Session Vault.

Phase 2: Deep Reconnaissance & Indicator Extraction
Once raw data is captured, distributed Celery workers process the payload across three parallel
extraction pipelines.
If a hidden service is unreachable (>45s timeout), the controller switches to the
MinIO Snapshot Replay cache.
What is Being Scanned / Probed:
Dark web marketplace vendor pages, forum discussion threads, profile biography
blocks, and paste dumps.
 DOCX
HTTP response headers, hidden service mirror lists, and robots.txt files.
Inputs Required: Target .onion URL, authentication credentials (if gated forum), initial
seed keywords, or analyst-submitted raw dump archives.
 DOCX
Outputs Produced: Compressed raw HTML artifacts and HTTP response header blobs
stored in MinIO with SHA-256 integrity hashes, alongside queued Celery job IDs.
DOCX
                    ┌── ►  [Worker A: Infra Probe] ── ►  Headers, JARM, mod_status
                    │
[Queued Payloads] ───┼── ►  [Worker B: Crypto/Key]   ── ►  PGP RFC 4880, UTXO Cluster
                    │
                    └── ►  [Worker C: AI Stylometry] ── ►  Fast-DetectGPT, Embeddings
Analyst Action (User Journey): Passive monitoring. The analyst watches the Ingestion
Pipeline progress bar on the dashboard as the system extracts entities from raw
unstructured dark web text.
 DOCX
Frontend UI State:
Indicator processing cards show real-time extraction feeds: 
New PGP Key Detected, Bitcoin Transaction Ingested, 
Origin Server Suspected.
Visual badge alerts highlight anomalies (e.g., red badge for mod_status Exposed,
yellow badge for Mixer Hop Identified).
Backend Execution:
Worker A (Infrastructure): Executes HTTP GET probes for misconfiguration vectors (
/server-status, /server-info, .git/HEAD, .env). Extracts TLS certificates,
generates 62-character JARM fingerprints, and calculates MurmurHash3 favicon
values. Dispatches passive queries to Censys and Shodan APIs to identify candidate

=== PAGE 45 ===
Phase 3: Graph Ingestion, Knowledge Modeling & Correlation
The platform structures extracted indicators into a unified graph and executes asymmetric
correlation scoring.
clearnet IP addresses. Resolves candidate IPs against BGP routing tables to
distinguish CDN edge proxies from real origin hosting servers.
Worker B (Cryptographic & Financial): Parses ASCII-armored PGP keys via RFC 4880
specifications, extracting 160-bit SHA-1 or 256-bit SHA-2 Master Fingerprints while
discarding 8-byte short IDs. Ingests crypto addresses; evaluates Bitcoin transactions
through the Common-Input Ownership heuristic while passing transactions through
a CoinJoin taint filter (  inputs/outputs) to prevent false-positive wallet merges.
Stores Monero addresses for off-chain temporal correlation.
≥ 5
DOCX+ 1
Worker C (AI NLP & Profiling): Filters out quotes, code blocks, and system signatures.
Passes clean text through a Fast-DetectGPT perplexity gate; if the text is flagged as
machine-translated or LLM-sanitized, stylometric scoring is suppressed. Otherwise,
it extracts 400+ Writeprints features (Yule’s K, Simpson’s D, function-word
distributions) and computes 1024-dimensional semantic embeddings using Siamese
RoBERTa. Computes operational intent topics via BERTopic.
 DOCX
What is Being Scanned / Probed:
Exposed web server status pages and environment configurations.
OpenSSL handshakes, cipher-suite negotiations, and binary static assets (
favicon.ico).
PGP key blocks, Bitcoin/Monero addresses, and unstructured natural language posts.
DOCX
Inputs Required: Raw HTML, server response headers, extracted text blobs, and public
blockchain ledger data via RPC.
Outputs Produced: Structured indicator JSON payloads containing parsed PGP
fingerprints, wallet clusters, JARM hashes, clearnet IP candidates, and stylometric vector
arrays.
 DOCX
DOCX
 
[Extracted Indicators] ── ►  [Neo4j Graph Engine] ── ►  [Asymmetric Scorer] ── ►  [Ranked P
Analyst Action (User Journey): The analyst receives a system notification: "High-
Confidence Attribution Candidates Identified for Case CASE-2026-X01." They open the 
Investigation Workspace to explore auto-generated entity correlations.
 DOCX

discarding 8-byte short IDs. Ingests crypto addresses; evaluates Bitcoin transactions
through the Common-Input Ownership heuristic while passing transactions through
a CoinJoin taint filter (  inputs/outputs) to prevent false-positive wallet merges.
Stores Monero addresses for off-chain temporal correlation.
≥ 5
DOCX+ 1
Worker C (AI NLP & Profiling): Filters out quotes, code blocks, and system signatures.
Passes clean text through a Fast-DetectGPT perplexity gate; if the text is flagged as
machine-translated or LLM-sanitized, stylometric scoring is suppressed. Otherwise,
it extracts 400+ Writeprints features (Yule’s K, Simpson’s D, function-word
distributions) and computes 1024-dimensional semantic embeddings using Siamese
RoBERTa. Computes operational intent topics via BERTopic.
 DOCX
What is Being Scanned / Probed:
Exposed web server status pages and environment configurations.
OpenSSL handshakes, cipher-suite negotiations, and binary static assets (
favicon.ico).
PGP key blocks, Bitcoin/Monero addresses, and unstructured natural language posts.
DOCX
Inputs Required: Raw HTML, server response headers, extracted text blobs, and public
blockchain ledger data via RPC.
Outputs Produced: Structured indicator JSON payloads containing parsed PGP
fingerprints, wallet clusters, JARM hashes, clearnet IP candidates, and stylometric vector
arrays.

confirms or rejects links.

The Persona List View populates with unified persona cards displaying primary
handle, observed aliases, post volume, last-seen timestamp, and a summary of
known identifiers.
 DOCX

UNVERIFIED).

Cypher ingestion jobs map indicators into Neo4j: ThreatActor, ForumHandle, 
PGPKey, CryptoWallet, OnionService, and OriginServer.
 DOCX
Deterministic Merging: If two forum handles share an identical master PGP
fingerprint or belong to the same non-mixer Bitcoin input-spend cluster, an
automated Cypher transaction binds both handles under a single ThreatActor
cluster node.
 DOCX

hard overrides ( ) to deterministic signals, while soft probabilistic signals
(stylometric cosine similarity, BERTopic overlap, UTC diurnal activity) are capped at 
 max.

On Confirm Link: An API call executes a Neo4j Cypher update, merging the
handles under a unified ThreatActor cluster and adding an edge labeled 
[:CONFIRMED_ALIASED_AS].

[:DO_NOT_MERGE]), preventing this link pairing from reappearing in future
candidate queues.

Phase 5: Forensic Timeline Exploration & Legal Verification
The analyst contextualizes the confirmed threat actor across time and validates infrastructure
leads against legal scanning boundaries.
Outputs Produced: Updated cluster topology in Neo4j, status change event emitted via
WebSockets to other active analysts, and an immutable log entry in PostgreSQL.
 DOCX
 
[Interactive Graph Canvas] ── ►  [Temporal Time Slider] ── ►  [Warrant-Gated Verification
Analyst Action (User Journey):
The analyst switches to the Interactive Graph Canvas (React Flow / Cytoscape.js).
DOCX
They scrub the Temporal Timeline Slider from 2024-01-01 to Current Date to
visualize how the actor migrated between handles, changed PGP keys, and shifted
hosting infrastructure.
If the analyst needs to confirm an unverified clearnet IP, they toggle Active Host
Verification.
The system prompts for a judicial warrant or case authorization ID before sending
active probe packets to the candidate clearnet IP.
Frontend UI State:
The node graph animates dynamically as the timeline slider moves: older retired
handles fade to grey, newly registered wallets highlight in green, and active
infrastructure nodes glow orange.
Clicking an edge between a handle and a wallet exposes transaction hashes, block
timestamps, and input amounts.
The Legal Verification Gateway modal prompts: "Input Case Authorization Number
to unlock direct SNI/Host header validation against candidate origin server IP."
Backend Execution:
Queries Neo4j using temporal Cypher queries filtering by first_seen and 
last_seen timestamps.
Legal Gateway Verification: Validates the analyst's role in PostgreSQL. If authorized,
the backend fires a single, strictly scoped HTTP HEAD request with the target's Host
header directly to the candidate non-CDN IP to confirm response parity with the 
.onion front-end.
 DOCX

Outputs Produced: Time-sequenced sub-graph representations and direct HTTP origin-
response parity verification records.

Use lfnovo/open-notebook if your primary requirement is complete data sovereignty or
multi-model flexibility (like running local LLMs via Ollama or using Claude 3.5 Sonnet). Stick with 
Google NotebookLM if you want zero infrastructure maintenance, free access to Gemini’s
massive 1M+ token context window, and top-tier audio summaries.

=== PAGE 53 ===
Stay with Google NotebookLM if:
The Best Alternatives to Consider
What types of documents are you primarily analyzing, and is local data privacy an absolute
requirement for your workflow?
You handle strictly confidential documents, legal evidence, internal codebases, or
proprietary IP that cannot leave your network.
You prefer using models like Claude 3.5 Sonnet or local offline models (Llama 3, DeepSeek)
over Gemini.
You need programmatic API access to query your notebooks from external scripts or
internal tools.
You want holistic synthesis across massive text dumps (e.g., 20 PDFs totaling 400,000
words). Gemini’s raw context window handles global cross-document thematic synthesis
significantly better than standard vector-chunked RAG.
You do not want to manage Docker containers, database migrations, or server resources.
The "Audio Overview" feature is a core part of your daily consumption workflow.
AnythingLLM (Mintplex Labs): The most practical alternative if you want local privacy
without Docker complexity. It provides an all-in-one desktop installer
(Mac/Windows/Linux), built-in local vector database, flexible model switching (local or
cloud), and zero-configuration document RAG.
Open-WebUI: The gold standard for self-hosted LLM interfaces. If you run Ollama, Open-
WebUI’s built-in "Knowledge" collections provide a robust, actively maintained RAG
pipeline with granular role-based permissions and multi-user support.
RAGFlow: The best option for deeply complex, academic, or financial documents. It uses
visual document parsing (DeepDoc) to extract tables, charts, and dense layouts that
standard text parsers typically break.

=== PAGE 55 ===
Here is an objective, engineering-focused breakdown of where this table is accurate, where it is
misleading, and the major architectural factor it completely leaves out.
1. Where the Table is 100% Accurate (Open Notebook Wins)
2. Where the Table is Misleading or Overstated
A. The "Podcast Speakers" Comparison (Flexibility vs. Quality)
B. The "Cost" Comparison (Pay-as-you-go vs. Free)
Data Sovereignty & Privacy: If you run local models via Ollama or LM Studio, your
sensitive documents, proprietary code, or investigative files never leave your machine.
NotebookLM runs entirely on Google's cloud.
Model Agnosticism: You are not locked into Gemini. You can switch between Claude 3.5
Sonnet for coding and reasoning, DeepSeek for cost efficiency, or local quantized models
for offline work.
API Automation: Google NotebookLM has no official public API. If you need to integrate
document analysis into a script, Celery worker, or enterprise pipeline, Open Notebook’s
REST API makes this possible.
No Vendor Lock-In: You own the code, the underlying SurrealDB database, and the data
schema.
The Claim: Open Notebook claims an advantage with "1–4 speakers with custom profiles"
versus NotebookLM's "2 speakers only."
The Reality:
Open Notebook uses scripted text-to-speech (TTS) (e.g., ElevenLabs, Kokoro, Edge-
TTS). The output sounds like distinct robotic or synthetic voices reading lines off a
teleprompter.
NotebookLM’s Deep Dive Audio Overview uses a specialized end-to-end
conversational audio model. It generates natural human banter, interruptions, sighs,
pitch modulation, and spontaneous conversational pacing that standard multi-voice
TTS scripts cannot match.
Takeaway: Open Notebook gives you more control over the speaker count;
NotebookLM provides a vastly more natural listening experience.
The Claim: Open Notebook claims an advantage with "Pay only for AI usage" versus
NotebookLM's "Free tier + Monthly subscription."
The Reality:
Google NotebookLM is currently completely free for standard Google accounts,
allowing up to 50 sources per notebook (up to 500,000 words per source)

=== PAGE 56 ===
C. Deployment & Maintenance Overhead
3. The Critical Omission: Long-Context Processing vs. Vector RAG
The table omits the most significant technical difference between the two systems: how they
ingest and read documents.
4. Summary Verdict: Which Should You Use?
processed by Gemini 1.5 Pro without API billing.
With Open Notebook, if you upload multiple dense documents and run iterative
queries using frontier cloud models like Claude 3.5 Sonnet or GPT-4o, input token
costs accumulate rapidly, potentially costing several dollars per research session. If
running locally, you must provide your own GPU hardware (VRAM).
The Claim: "Deploy anywhere (Docker, cloud, local)" listed as an unqualified advantage.
The Reality: Self-hosting introduces maintenance overhead: managing Docker containers,
handling SurrealDB database migrations, debugging port bindings, and managing local
LLM context limits and quantizations. NotebookLM is a zero-maintenance SaaS.
 
Google NotebookLM:
[ Document 1 ] + [ Document 2 ] + [ Document 3 ] ── ►  Entirely loaded into Gemini 1.5 
                                                     (No chunking, global reasoning 
Open Notebook:
[ Documents ] ── ►  Chunking (Docling) ── ►  Embeddings ── ►  SurrealDB Vector Search ── ►  T
                                                     (Subject to retrieval loss, bro
Google NotebookLM does not rely solely on traditional chunked vector search. It loads
documents directly into Gemini’s massive 1,000,000+ token context window. It can read
20 research papers or a 600-page book in full, allowing it to synthesize high-level themes,
locate subtle cross-document contradictions, and maintain global context.
Open Notebook relies on traditional RAG (Retrieval-Augmented Generation):
documents are chopped into text chunks, embedded into a vector database, and only the
top  most mathematically similar snippets are passed to the model.
k
Why this matters: If your research question requires connecting an idea on page 12 with a
consequence on page 418, chunk-based RAG often fails to retrieve both pieces
simultaneously, whereas a native million-token context window processes the whole
document at once.

To win Smart India Hackathon (Problem Statement ID: SIH26182) under the Ministry of Home
Affairs (MHA) and the Indian Cyber Crime Coordination Centre (I4C), the solution must
directly resolve the operational reality of Indian law enforcement officers rather than pitching
an academic blockchain explorer or an unaffordable Chainalysis wrapper.
When an Indian Investigating Officer (IO) receives a cyber fraud complaint via the National
Cyber Crime Reporting Portal (NCRP) or the 1930 Helpline (CFCFRMS), stolen fiat is rapidly
converted via P2P into cryptocurrency—predominantly USDT on Tron (TRC-20) or 
Ethereum/BNB (ERC-20/BEP-20). The suspect wallet is almost always an unhosted (non-
custodial) wallet.

To win Smart India Hackathon (Problem Statement ID: SIH26182) under the Ministry of Home
Affairs (MHA) and the Indian Cyber Crime Coordination Centre (I4C), the solution must
directly resolve the operational reality of Indian law enforcement officers rather than pitching
an academic blockchain explorer or an unaffordable Chainalysis wrapper.
When an Indian Investigating Officer (IO) receives a cyber fraud complaint via the National
Cyber Crime Reporting Portal (NCRP) or the 1930 Helpline (CFCFRMS), stolen fiat is rapidly
converted via P2P into cryptocurrency—predominantly USDT on Tron (TRC-20) or 
Ethereum/BNB (ERC-20/BEP-20). The suspect wallet is almost always an unhosted (non-
custodial) wallet.
=== PAGE 58 ===
An IO cannot issue a freezing order to a non-custodial wallet; they can only enforce a lawful
disclosure or freezing notice under Section 94 of the Bharatiya Nagarik Suraksha Sanhita
(BNSS), 2023 (formerly Section 91/102 CrPC) to a Virtual Asset Service Provider (VASP) via the 
SAHYOG Portal. If tracing takes 48 hours, the funds exit through off-shore cash-out hubs.
The end-to-end operational architecture below addresses this requirement with specific
mitigations for every technical and jurisdictional bottleneck.
Part 1: Evaluator Mindset (MHA / I4C, CIS Division)
The evaluation panel from I4C consists of senior police officers (SP/DSP ranks), technical
directors, and cyber forensics consultants. They will immediately fail teams that make the
following four errors:
                      ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
                      │    NCRP / 1930 Helpline / SAHYOG Investigation Portal    │
                      │           (Suspect Wallet Address / TX Hash Input)       │
                      └────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────┘
                                                   │
                                                   ▼
                      ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
                      │      Multi-Chain RPC & Indexing Layer (TVM, EVM, UTXO)   │
                      │         (TronGrid, Blockscout, Mempool, Solana RPC)      │
                      └────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────┘
                                                   │
                                                   ▼
                      ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐

                      │     (Bounded Pruning BFS + Sweeper Address Classifier)   │
                      └────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────┘
                                                   │
                                                   ▼
                      ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
                      │     Open VASP Attribution Cluster & FIU-IND Registry     │

                      └────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────┘

illegal loan apps, fake trading platforms) move via TRON (USDT-TRC20) and BNB Chain,

=== PAGE 59 ===
Part 2: Critical Edge Cases, Loopholes, and Defenses
1. Peeling Chains & Combinatorial Graph Explosion (Smurfing)
2. Cross-Chain Bridges & DEX Swapping (SunSwap, Uniswap, ThorChain)
not Bitcoin. If a demo only supports Bitcoin UTXO tracing, it does not match I4C’s daily
caseload.
Confusing Exchange Deposit Wallets with Hot Wallets: Centralized exchanges (e.g.,
Binance, CoinDCX, KuCoin) generate unique, temporary Deposit Forwarding Addresses
for each customer, which subsequently sweep funds into centralized Exchange
Hot/Omnibus Wallets. A notice cannot ask an exchange to "freeze the Binance Hot
Wallet"—that freezes omnibus liquidity. The tool must trace back to the specific customer
deposit address to identify the KYC-verified suspect.
2.
The "Commercial API Wrapper" Trap: Pitching an expensive commercial API (Chainalysis,
TRM Labs) fails because government agencies already evaluate these solutions. The
proposal must demonstrate an open, locally hosted attribution and graph traversal engine.
3.
Ignoring the Legal & Portal Context: Tracing is only half the problem; asset recovery is
the goal. The system must natively format outputs for direct submission to the SAHYOG
Portal under Section 94 BNSS.
4.
The Loophole: Threat actors distribute 10,000 USDT across hundreds of intermediary
micro-wallets in 10-hop peeling chains. A naive Breadth-First Search (BFS) graph traversal
causes an exponential combinatorial explosion ( ), freezing backend servers.
O(b )d
The Mitigation: Implement Heuristic-Driven Bounded Pruning BFS with a Dynamic Taint-
Flow Model (Modified Haircut/Poison Score):
Ignore dust outputs below gas-cost thresholds ( ).
< 5 USDT
Track transaction paths using a minimum volume threshold (  of original stolen
amount).
≥ 5%
Automatically stop forward exploration along any path the moment it hits a known
VASP cluster, marking that node as a Terminal Nearest VASP Sink.
The Loophole: Criminals swap Tron USDT for Monero (XMR) or bridge Tron USDT to
Ethereum via cross-chain bridges, severing basic single-chain address lookups.
The Mitigation:
Integrate smart-contract event parsers across EVM and TVM (TRON Virtual Machine)
to decode standard Transfer, Swap, and BridgeDeposit logs.
Tag decentralized liquidity pools and bridges as INTERMEDIARY_DEFI_ROUTER.

=== PAGE 60 ===
3. Mixer / Privacy Pool Taint Handling
4. FIU-IND Compliance & Cross-Border Jurisdiction
Part 3: System Architecture & Technical Stack
Extract destination recipient wallet parameters directly from bridge contract calls
(e.g., Stargate, Across Protocol, deBridge) to resume tracing on the destination chain
without manual intervention.
The Loophole: Threat actors direct funds into mixers (Tornado Cash, CoinJoin, Railgun)
before moving to an exchange.
The Mitigation: The engine identifies the deposit transaction into the mixer contract and
flags the downstream wallet as HIGH_RISK_MIXER_TAINTED. When the funds emerge
from relayers or unshielded pools, the platform uses temporal volume correlation (
, volume tolerance ) to surface probable egress links with calibrated
probabilistic confidence scores.
Δt <
2 hours ±2%
The Loophole: Identifying an exchange is ineffective if that exchange is an offshore entity
that refuses Section 94 BNSS notices issued by state police.
The Mitigation: The system integrates a database mapping FIU-IND Registered
Reporting Entities (e.g., CoinDCX, ZebPay, Mudrex, CoinSwitch) versus Offshore Non-
Compliant VASPs (e.g., offshore entities issued PMLA compliance notices).
If the nearest VASP is domestic/registered, it triggers an immediate Tier-1 Local
Direct Freeze protocol.
If the nearest VASP is an unregistered offshore exchange, it routes the alert to the 
I4C Central Interpol / Mutual Legal Assistance Treaty (MLAT) Cell with a
specialized cross-border evidence package.
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐

3. Mixer / Privacy Pool Taint Handling
4. FIU-IND Compliance & Cross-Border Jurisdiction
Part 3: System Architecture & Technical Stack
Extract destination recipient wallet parameters directly from bridge contract calls
(e.g., Stargate, Across Protocol, deBridge) to resume tracing on the destination chain
without manual intervention.
The Loophole: Threat actors direct funds into mixers (Tornado Cash, CoinJoin, Railgun)
before moving to an exchange.
The Mitigation: The engine identifies the deposit transaction into the mixer contract and
flags the downstream wallet as HIGH_RISK_MIXER_TAINTED. When the funds emerge
from relayers or unshielded pools, the platform uses temporal volume correlation (
, volume tolerance ) to surface probable egress links with calibrated
probabilistic confidence scores.
Δt <
2 hours ±2%
The Loophole: Identifying an exchange is ineffective if that exchange is an offshore entity
that refuses Section 94 BNSS notices issued by state police.
The Mitigation: The system integrates a database mapping FIU-IND Registered
Reporting Entities (e.g., CoinDCX, ZebPay, Mudrex, CoinSwitch) versus Offshore Non-
Compliant VASPs (e.g., offshore entities issued PMLA compliance notices).
If the nearest VASP is domestic/registered, it triggers an immediate Tier-1 Local
Direct Freeze protocol.
If the nearest VASP is an unregistered offshore exchange, it routes the alert to the 
I4C Central Interpol / Mutual Legal Assistance Treaty (MLAT) Cell with a
specialized cross-border evidence package.

└────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
Neo4j Graph Database: Relational databases (MySQL/Postgres) require nested recursive
SQL joins that time out when querying multi-hop transactions. Neo4j executes Cypher
graph traversals in milliseconds, scaling to 15+ transaction hops.
TronGrid + TVM Native Parsing: Tron accounts for the vast majority of retail cyber fraud
cases handled by Indian Cyber Crime Cells. Native TRC-20 smart-contract log filtering
provides real-time transaction ingestion.
Pre-Clustered Open VASP Database: The platform uses a local graph cluster of 1.2M+
tagged exchange hot wallets, sweeper addresses, and deposit forwarders, eliminating
recurring API query charges.
Case Inception (1930 / NCRP Intake):1.
The Investigating Officer (IO) receives an FIR or NCRP Acknowledgment number
(e.g., NCRP-2026-99104).
The victim’s initial transaction details are entered: Target Unhosted Wallet (
TX: 0x7c1a... or Tron address TYiR...) and transaction hash.
Automated Forward Path Tracing:2.
The engine deploys a bounded-pruning BFS graph walk, tracking the flow of funds
forward across multiple non-custodial intermediary wallets.
Intermediary wallets are tagged with risk flags (e.g., Mule Hop 1, 
Smart Contract Router, Consolidation Wallet).
Nearest VASP Identification:3.
At Hop 3, the engine identifies a transfer to a deposit address (0xabc4...) that
forwards directly into a verified Binance Centralized Sweeper Wallet (
