Based on the design specifications and architecture established in 📖  SIHSHADOWTRACER, the
platform operates across specific operational modes, event-driven pipeline triggers, and 
evidentiary threshold states to fulfill the problem statement.
1. Operational Modes (When the System Runs)
The platform runs across three distinct operating conditions:
Mode A: 24/7 Autonomous Continuous Gathering Mode (Proactive Background)
Mode B: On-Demand / Case-Driven Investigation Mode (Reactive Intake)
Mode C: Resilient Snapshot & Offline Replay Mode (Network Failover)
DOCX
DOCX
When it works: Continuously in the background without requiring manual human prompts
.
 DOCX
Execution: A distributed Celery scheduler dispatches periodic headless crawling tasks
through Stem-controlled Tor circuits. It continuously navigates registered darknet
marketplaces, leak sites, and forums (e.g., Dread, Exploit, Bohemia).
 DOCX+ 1
What it does: Scrapes vendor listings, user profiles, PGP key blocks, and forum discussion
threads, parsing them into real-time extraction queues. It also runs passive health checks
against hidden services to track their operational status.
 DOCX+ 1
When it works: Triggered when an investigating officer (IO) or analyst inputs a new target,
case identifier, or seed indicator.
 DOCX
Execution: Activated when an investigator submits an unindexed .onion URL, a specific
forum alias, a suspect Bitcoin/Monero address, or an offline dump archive (HTML/JSON
dumps).
 DOCX
What it does: The system immediately spins up dedicated scraper containers, prioritizes
the target in the Redis queue, and runs deep multi-hop relationship traversals in Neo4j to
find historical links across previously indexed forums.
 DOCX
When it works: Automatically activates when a target Tor hidden service goes offline,
drops circuits, rate-limits scrapers, or deploys heavy Proof-of-Work (PoW) captchas.
DOCX
Execution: If an .onion endpoint fails to respond within a 45-second timeout window,
the ingestion controller switches from live probing to the immutable snapshot replay
cache in MinIO.
 DOCX
What it does: Allows analysts and evaluators to continue feature extraction, graph
modeling, and attribution scoring without suffering demo halts or investigation stalls due

=== PAGE 66 ===
2. Event-Driven Pipeline Triggers (When Extraction & Scoring Wake Up)
The backend ingestion pipelines trigger automatically upon detecting specific evidentiary
indicators:
to darknet network latency. DOCX
DOCX
 
[ New Forum Post Ingested ] ── ►  Triggers Worker C (AI Stylometry & Topic Extraction)
[ Onion Endpoint Scanned ]  ── ►  Triggers Worker A (mod_status & JARM/Favicon Probes)
[ Crypto Address Parsed ]   ── ►  Triggers Worker B (UTXO Cluster & CoinJoin Taint Filt
[ Shared Key / IP Detected ] ── ►  Triggers Knowledge Graph Merge & Attribution Alert
Trigger 1: Infrastructure Misconfiguration Event:
When it fires: When the scanner encounters an open /server-status or 
/server-info page, unlinked .git/HEAD or .env files, or an active SSL
certificate served over Tor.
 DOCX
System Action: Extracts internal Apache client connection tables and clearnet
Subject Alternative Names (SANs). It computes the 62-character JARM fingerprint
and MurmurHash3 favicon hash, querying Censys and Shodan to identify matching
clearnet origin IPs.
 DOCX+ 1
Trigger 2: Cryptographic Key Publication Event:
When it fires: When a forum post or marketplace profile contains an ASCII-armored
PGP block.
 DOCX
System Action: The RFC 4880 parser extracts the unalterable 160-bit SHA-1 or 256-
bit SHA-2 Master Fingerprint. If that fingerprint already exists under a different
handle in Neo4j, it instantly fires a deterministic merge event.
 DOCX+ 1
Trigger 3: Blockchain Transaction Movement Event:
When it fires: When an unhosted Bitcoin address spends funds in a multi-input
transaction.
 DOCX
System Action: Applies the Common-Input Ownership Heuristic to cluster co-
spending addresses into a unified wallet entity.
 DOCX
Trigger 4: Persona Migration / Alias Rebranding Event:
When it fires: When an actor retires an old handle and surfaces under a new alias
without reusing keys or wallets.
 DOCX

3. Evidentiary Thresholds: When the Platform Declares a Match
The scoring engine applies an asymmetric heuristic model, determining attribution confidence
based on mathematical and evidentiary criteria:
Operational
Scenario
Triggering Conditions Resulting System State
High-
Confidence
Attribution (
)
• Exact 40-character PGP master key
fingerprint match across different
forums.
• Common-input Bitcoin wallet spend
without mixer taint.
• Leaked clearnet IP via Apache 
mod_status confirmed on non-CDN
ASN.
Automatic Cluster Merge: Neo4j
merges handles under a single 
(:ThreatActor) cluster and
notifies the analyst workspace.
Medium-
Confidence
Candidate (
)
• JARM hash + favicon match without
direct IP exposure.
• High stylometric Siamese similarity (
) + shared BERTopic domain intent.
• Matching contact handles (Telegram,
Jabber).
Human Review Queue: Links are
flagged for manual review and
cannot auto-merge without analyst
sign-off.
Suppression /
Withheld
Linkage ( )
• CoinJoin mixer transaction detected (
 inputs/outputs).
• Text flagged as 
ADVERSARIALLY_SANITIZED via
Fast-DetectGPT (LLM or translation loop)
.
• Clearnet IP belongs to a
Cloudflare/Fastly CDN edge proxy ASN.
Safety Guard: Suppresses
automatic attribution, tags nodes
as (:MixerHop) or 
(:EdgeProxy), and forces
technical corroboration.
System Action: Triggers the Siamese RoBERTa network and BERTopic pipeline to

4. Human-in-the-Loop Lifecycle: When the Analyst Steps In
The platform engages the investigator at three distinct decision checkpoints:
DOCX
DOCX
At Link Review: When the engine generates a ranked correlation candidate, the analyst
opens the Side-by-Side Signal Dissector to inspect textual diffs, cryptographic
fingerprints, and infrastructure matches before confirming or rejecting the link[cite: 1].
1.
At Legal Verification: When a candidate clearnet IP is discovered, active origin verification
(direct Host/SNI header probing) remains locked until an authorized analyst inputs a valid
judicial warrant or statutory case ID[cite: 1].
2.
At Case Dissemination: Once an investigation is complete, the case file enters a Senior
Analyst Approval Gate[cite: 1]. The platform requires senior digital sign-off before
rendering and releasing exportable CSV IOC feeds, STIX 2.1 JSON packages, or court-
defensible PDF dossiers[cite: 1].

It ingests raw marketplace listings, vendor profiles, discussion threads, and
descriptor metadata directly into the processing pipeline.
 DOCX
Problem Statement Clause:"Finding misconfigurations in Tor hidden
services—such as exposed server-status pages, SSL certificates
tied to clearnet domains, default service banners, descriptor
inconsistencies, etc and matching them with clearnet
infrastructure to point to the likely origin servers."
When It Works: Triggered automatically whenever a target .onion service is scanned or
modified.
 DOCX
Operational Actions:
Exposed Status & Information Endpoints: Probes target .onion URLs for exposed 
/server-status (Apache), /server-info, .git/HEAD, or .env files. If
exposed, it extracts client connection tables, internal IPs, and vhost configurations.
DOCX+ 1
SSL/TLS Certificate Leaks: Captures SSL/TLS certificates presented over Tor. If the
certificate's Common Name (CN) or Subject Alternative Names (SAN) leak a clearnet
domain or a unique SHA-256 fingerprint, the system extracts it.
 DOCX+ 1
Cryptographic Stack Fingerprinting: Generates 62-character JARM TLS client-hello
signatures and MurmurHash3 favicon hashes.
 DOCX
Clearnet Correlation: Queries Censys and Shodan APIs using these fingerprints to
match the .onion site to matching public clearnet IP addresses and physical origin
servers.
 DOCX
Problem Statement Clause:"Mapping threat actors across multiple
marketplaces into a single relationship graph of handles, PGP
keys, wallets and trust links."
When It Works: Triggered whenever cryptographic keys, cryptocurrency addresses, or
contact handles appear in scraped data.
 DOCX
Operational Actions:
PGP Key Re-use: Parses raw OpenPGP ASCII blocks using RFC 4880 to extract
immutable 160-bit SHA-1 or 256-bit SHA-2 Master Key Fingerprints. If two handles on
different marketplaces share the same master key, the system automatically
correlates them.

Cryptocurrency Transaction Clustering: Evaluates Bitcoin and multi-chain wallet
addresses. It applies the Common-Input Ownership heuristic to cluster disparate
input addresses spent in the same transaction under a unified wallet cluster (while
filtering out CoinJoin mixers).
 DOCX+ 1
Knowledge Graph Mapping: Maps all entities as nodes (ThreatActor, 
ForumHandle, PGPKey, CryptoWallet, OriginServer) and directed edges (
POSTED_AS, USES_KEY, CONTROLS_WALLET, ASSOCIATED_IP) inside a Neo4j
property graph.
 DOCX
Problem Statement Clause:"Using AI-based analysis, including
stylometric persona identification and behavioural profiling, to
link rebranded or migrated personas to known threat actors."
When It Works: Triggered when a new alias surfaces, an existing vendor disappears (exit-
scams or rebrands), or an alias has no matching PGP keys or wallet history.
 DOCX
Operational Actions:
Adversarial Pre-Filtering: Fast-DetectGPT checks text for machine-translation loops
or generative LLM rewriting to ensure stylometric integrity.
 DOCX
Writeprints Feature Extraction: Measures vocabulary richness (Yule’s K, Simpson’s
D), punctuation anomalies, sentence distributions, and function-word frequencies
across 400+ dimensions.
 DOCX
Deep Siamese Metric Learning: Passes token sequences through a fine-tuned
Siamese Transformer (RoBERTa) to calculate cosine similarity against historical threat
actor writing vectors.
 DOCX
Behavioral & Topic Profiling: Uses BERTopic to determine domain intent (e.g.,
specific exploit kits, bulletproof hosting, carding formats) combined with UTC diurnal
activity schedules.
 DOCX
Asymmetric Confidence Scoring: Merges soft signals with a ceiling cap (maximum 
 confidence) to ensure probabilistic AI matches route to human analyst
verification rather than false auto-attributions.

=== PAGE 72 ===
Scanning every user or every connection on the Tor network 24/7 is both technically
impossible and legally impermissible:
The Real Architecture: A Hybrid Dual-Engine Model
The problem statement requires both continuous gathering and targeted querying through an
analytical dashboard. The platform achieves this through a Hybrid Dual-Engine Architecture:
Engine 1: What Runs 24/7 Autonomously (The Intelligence Lake)
Technical Impossibility: Tor is an encrypted onion-routing network with millions of client
circuits. Client users do not expose open IP ports to be scanned. Furthermore, Tor v3 
.onion addresses are 56-character Ed25519 public keys that cannot be brute-forced or
enumerated via a public directory.
Legal Boundary: Attempting to monitor all network users amounts to unauthorized mass
interception and wiretapping.
DOCX

Engine 2: What Runs After a Complaint Is Lodged (Targeted Investigation)
When an Investigating Officer (IO) receives an FIR or a complaint from the National Cyber
Crime Reporting Portal (NCRP) / 1930 Helpline, they enter the suspect’s specific indicator into
the platform:
How to Explain This to the SIH Jury (30-Second Pitch)
Server headers, JARM signatures, and exposed status pages of active marketplace 
.onion sites.
 DOCX
Result: It continuously builds and updates a centralized Knowledge Graph (Neo4j) before
any specific complaint is assigned to an officer.
 DOCX
The Input: A suspect unhosted wallet address (e.g., USDT TRC-20), a transaction hash, a
forum alias, or an extortion .onion link.
The Execution:
Instant Lake Query: The system queries the pre-indexed Neo4j database.1. DOCX
If already indexed: The IO receives an immediate attribution match (e.g., "This
wallet was posted on Dread 3 weeks ago by handle 'KryptonBroker', matching
PGP Key X and Origin Server IP Y").
1
1 DOCX
On-Demand Targeted Probe (If New / Unseen):2.
If the .onion link or handle is not in the database, the system spins up an on-
demand focused crawler to probe that specific site for misconfigurations (
mod_status, JARM, SSL leaks).
1
1 DOCX
If it is an unhosted wallet, the forward-path traversal engine traces the money
forward across intermediary hops until it hits the Nearest VASP (Virtual Asset
Service Provider) to generate a freezing order under Section 94 BNSS.
2
2
"Our platform does not perform illegal or impossible mass
surveillance on all Tor users. Instead, it operates on a Hybrid
Intelligence Model:
1. In the background, our autonomous engine continuously indexes public
illicit marketplaces, forums, and leak surfaces, cataloging PGP keys, wallet
clusters, and server infrastructure fingerprints into a graph database.
2. When an Investigating Officer receives a complaint, they input the
suspect's handle, wallet, or .onion link. The system runs an instant
multi-hop graph match against the pre-indexed intelligence lake. If it's a
new indicator, it triggers an on-demand targeted deep scan to
deanonymize the origin server or trace the funds to the nearest exchange
for asset recovery."

DONT REFRENCE FROM TEH DOCX DOCUMENT EXPLAINM
EHOW IT IS WORKING DIFFERNRCE BETWEEN DARK WEB
HOW WE USING FOR NEW PREPATATORS HOW WE
ONITRORING THEM AND CATINCHING THEM WHAT ABUT
To understand how this platform actually works, you have to break through the biggest
misconception in cybercrime investigations: tracking a dark web criminal is almost never
about tracking their IP address.
On the dark web, IP addresses are designed to be useless. Tor routes traffic through three
encrypted hops (Guard node  Middle relay  Exit or Rendezvous point). Every 10 minutes,
the circuit changes. The user gets a new virtual path, and the server’s real IP is hidden behind a
cryptographic .onion address.
If an investigator relies on "finding their IP address" or "tracking their handle," they will fail.
Threat actors are humans, and humans cannot change their operational habits, their
subconscious linguistic patterns, their server deployment scripts, or their need to cash out
cryptocurrency into real-world money.
1. Clearnet vs. Dark Web: Why the Approach is Different
→ →
Feature Clearnet Tracking (Normal
Web)
Dark Web Tracking (Our
Platform's Approach)
Primary Identifier Client IP, ISP subscriber logs,
browser cookies.
Cryptographic keys,
behavioral fingerprints, and
transaction flows.
Server Location DNS A-records pointing
directly to an IP.
Hidden behind Tor Rendezvous
circuits. Requires unmasking
via server misconfigurations
(e.g., status leaks, SSL
anomalies).
Identity Persistence Real email, phone number,
device MAC address.
Pseudonymous handles,
burner accounts, rotating PGP
keys.

On the dark web, IP addresses are designed to be useless. Tor routes traffic through three
encrypted hops (Guard node  Middle relay  Exit or Rendezvous point). Every 10 minutes,
the circuit changes. The user gets a new virtual path, and the server’s real IP is hidden behind a
cryptographic .onion address.
If an investigator relies on "finding their IP address" or "tracking their handle," they will fail.
Threat actors are humans, and humans cannot change their operational habits, their
subconscious linguistic patterns, their server deployment scripts, or their need to cash out
cryptocurrency into real-world money.
1. Clearnet vs. Dark Web: Why the Approach is Different
→ →

2. How the Platform Catches NEW Perpetrators (First-Time Threat Actors)
A "new perpetrator" is someone who recently appeared on a forum or launched a new .onion
shop. They have no prior record in the database.
The platform catches them through Autonomous Monitoring and Active Traps:
A. Infrastructure Mistakes (The Deployment Leak)
New actors are usually skilled at coding or buying exploits, but terrible at system administration.
When setting up a dark web server (Apache, Nginx):

Status Page Leaks: They forget to disable Apache’s mod_status or leave .git/HEAD
and .env accessible. The autonomous scanner probes the .onion site, hits 
http://[onion]/server-status, and directly reads the clearnet IP address
connecting to the server.
1.
SSL / TLS Certificate Leaks: They install an SSL certificate on their server meant for a
clearnet domain. The scanner grabs the TLS certificate presented over Tor, extracts the
Subject Alternative Name (SAN), and reveals their public domain (e.g., api.myshop.com).
2.

=== PAGE 76 ===
B. The Financial Trap (The Cash-Out Bottleneck)
A criminal does not commit cybercrime for fun; they do it for money.
3. How to Catch OLD Users / "Cheaters" Who Changed EVERYTHING
This is the central challenge: An experienced threat actor exit-scams, discards their handle,
deletes their PGP key, uses a brand new crypto wallet, and operates from a different
location. How do you prove "Alias B" is actually "Alias A"?
Everything on the surface changes, but underneath, five structural anchors remain:
Hardware & TLS Fingerprints (JARM): The scanner sends custom TLS packets to generate
a 62-character JARM hash and a Favicon hash. It queries global scanners
(Censys/Shodan). If the actor runs their .onion and their clearnet staging server on the
same machine, both return identical JARM and favicon hashes, exposing the hosting
provider and IP.
3.
The new actor posts a receiving wallet (Bitcoin, Tron USDT-TRC20, Ethereum).1.
The platform's crawlers automatically grab this address.2.
The moment a victim pays or the actor spends, the platform tracks the transaction chain
forward.
3.
The moment those funds touch an intermediary wallet that sweeps into a Centralized
Exchange (VASP) like Binance or CoinDCX, the exchange holds their KYC identity (PAN
card, Aadhaar, Passport, Bank Account).
4.
The platform generates an automated Section 94 BNSS Freezing and KYC Disclosure
Order, freezing their account at the exchange level before they can withdraw cash.
5.

                 │     "NEW" ALIAS (Old Criminal Rebranded)  │

                                      │
        ┌───────────────┬─────────────┴─┬───────────────┬───────────────┐
        ▼                ▼                ▼                ▼                ▼

=== PAGE 77 ===
1. AI Stylometry: Their Subconscious "Linguistic DNA"
People can change their names, but they cannot easily alter their subconscious writing habits.
2. The Blockchain Common-Input Trap (UTXO Clustering)
An actor can generate a hundred new, clean Bitcoin addresses. But eventually, they want to
spend their accumulated wealth.
3. Diurnal Activity Profiling (The Biological Clock)
Threat actors can mask their IP address, but they cannot mask human biology:
                                        ▼
                  [Neo4j Knowledge Graph: Identity Match]
Writeprints & Lexical Habit: The platform analyzes 400+ mathematical features of their
writing:
Do they use Oxford commas?
What is their vocabulary richness (Yule’s K index)?
How frequently do they use specific function words (whereas, although,
nevertheless)?
What are their unique punctuation quirks (e.g., using :) vs :-), double dashes 
--, specific capitalization errors)?
Deep Neural Embeddings: A fine-tuned transformer model (Siamese RoBERTa) turns
forum posts into mathematical vectors. Even if the actor changes their handle from 
KryptonBroker to NullPointer, the model calculates a stylometric cosine distance.
If their linguistic vectors cluster tightly together, the platform alerts the analyst to a
suspected identity migration.
The Common-Input Ownership Heuristic: If an actor needs to make a large purchase or
consolidate funds, their wallet software combines multiple past wallet balances into a
single transaction.
The Trap: The moment their new "clean" address and an old address from their previous
alias are used together as inputs in transaction , the blockchain mathematically proves
that the same entity holds the private keys to both addresses. The platform’s graph
engine instantly merges the two personas.
T  
x
Every time an alias posts, replies, or lists an item, the platform logs the timestamp
normalized to UTC.

=== PAGE 78 ===
4. Operational Tradecraft & Domain Intent (BERTopic)
Old habits persist in criminal business models:
5. Server Configuration Clones (Infrastructure Fingerprints)
When an actor sets up a new marketplace or service to replace their old one, they rarely build it
from scratch. They reuse their own Dockerfiles, deployment scripts, and preferred software
stacks.
4. How the Two Work Together: Summary Matrix
Over 50–100 interactions, a clear diurnal curve (active vs. sleeping hours) emerges.
If Old_Handle was active exclusively between 18:00 UTC and 03:00 UTC (indicating
Eastern Europe / Russia timezones) and New_Handle suddenly surfaces with the exact
same activity and sleep window, it provides strong corroborative behavioral proof.
Terms of Service & Rules: Darknet vendors frequently copy-paste or slightly modify their
own refund policies, dead-drop guarantees, encryption requirements, and escrow terms.
Specialized Inventory: If an actor previously sold access to specific industrial SCADA
networks or specialized zero-day exploits, and a "brand-new" seller appears offering the
exact same obscure access with identical file packaging conventions, topic modeling
(BERTopic) flags them as a functional match.
The TLS implementation, cipher-suite ordering, and TCP packet responses create an
identical JARM hash.
If their old seized site and their new "clean" site share a unique, rare JARM signature that
only 0.001% of the internet uses, they are deploying from the exact same private server
image.

WHAT IF THE NEW USER DIDNT LEAVE ANY GAPS ANY OF
YOU MENTIONED THEN WHAT?
The Hard Truth First
If a new threat actor executes 100% flawless operational security (OpSec), no automated
tool or passive scanner in the world can deanonymize them on Day 1.
If an actor does all of the following:
Passive scanning hits a mathematical wall. Tor’s onion routing and cryptography work as
designed. If there is zero leakage, passive correlation yields an attribution confidence of 0.00.
Any vendor or project claiming an AI can "crack" an actor in this state without active interaction
is fabricating capabilities.
However, in real-world cybercrime investigations, flawless passive OpSec is not an
impenetrable shield. Cybercriminals cannot operate in a vacuum—they must host their service
somewhere, deliver digital or physical goods, communicate with buyers, and convert proceeds.
Runs a hardened Tor v3 service on an isolated operating system with all status pages,
debugging endpoints, and banner disclosures disabled.
1.
Generates an ephemeral PGP key with zero metadata and discards it regularly.2.
Transacts exclusively in Monero (XMR) using one-time subaddresses.3.
Rewrites all text using a local LLM or keeps text to 5-word bullet points.4.
Employs default TLS cipher suites matching hundreds of thousands of standard
Debian/Nginx installations.
5.
By fusing Infrastructure Reconnaissance (finding setup errors), Blockchain Graph Analysis
(following the money), and AI Stylometric Profiling (identifying the human author), changing an
IP address or a username is no longer enough for an actor to remain anonymous.

The system does not fail or guess when an actor leaves no traces:

The "Cold Ghost Node" Creation:
The platform creates an isolated (:UnresolvedTarget) node in Neo4j. It logs the 
.onion address, the exact public PGP key, the listing timestamps, and the Monero
subaddress.
1.
Confidence Ceiling Enforcement:
Because all deterministic and corroborative signals are absent, the scoring engine
enforces a score of . The UI flags the target with a dark-grey badge: 
HARDENED_TARGET / ZERO_SIGNAL.
2.
0.00
Suppression of Hallucinations:
The Fast-DetectGPT and token-variance gates detect short or LLM-sanitized writing and
lock stylometric similarity to zero, preventing false associations with unrelated suspects.
3.
Automated Tripwire Placement:
The system schedules continuous, low-profile probes (every 15–30 minutes) to monitor for
the first infrastructure lapse (e.g., a server reboot that temporarily restores default configs
or exposes a port).

=== PAGE 81 ===
Phase 2: The 5 Vectors to Catch a "Zero-Gap" Threat Actor
When passive analysis returns zero, investigators force gaps through operational friction:
Vector 1: The Active Canary Trap & Payload Forensics (Controlled Delivery)
A vendor on the dark web must deliver a product—whether it is stolen data, malware, access
credentials, or contraband.
Vector 2: Side-Channel Timing & Infrastructure Outage Correlation
Even if the operating system exposes no status pages, the physical server is bound to the
physics of the data center hosting it.
Digital Goods (Data Dumps, Exploit Kits, Tools):
The Investigating Officer (IO) conducts an undercover purchase using law-
enforcement-controlled cryptocurrency.
The vendor provides a download link or an archive (.zip, .rar, .7z).
Deep Binary / Document Forensics:
Compiler Artifacts: If the actor wrote custom software or compiled a payload,
the binary often leaks debug paths (e.g., 
C:\Users\Sergey\source\repos\...), compilation timestamps, PDB paths,
language-pack identifiers, or Rich Header hashes (linking the compiler
environment to other known malware campaigns).
Archive Metadata: Archive headers reveal the local OS file system, exact
timezone of compression, and unpacked file permissions.
Canary Web-Bug Injection: If interacting via negotiation chats or ticketing
systems, investigators submit files containing outbound canary tokens (e.g., a
weaponized PDF or document with an embedded external resource request). If
opened on an unshielded workstation outside of a strict Tor sandbox, the target
machine resolves the external asset, leaking their true residential/VPN IP
address.
Physical Goods (Contraband, Hardware Tokens, SIMs):
The package must enter the physical mail or dead-drop stream.
Origin postmarks, drop-off location surveillance footage, packaging materials, and
latent forensic evidence bypass digital encryption entirely.
Clock-Skew Analysis (TCP Timestamp Fingerprinting):
Computer hardware clocks drift slightly based on temperature, voltage, and
hardware oscillator quirks.

=== PAGE 82 ===
Vector 3: The Hosting Infrastructure Payment Trail
A threat actor does not build their own physical server infrastructure or run their own undersea
fiber cables. They rent a Virtual Private Server (VPS) or dedicated box from a hosting provider.
Vector 4: The Counterparty & Upstream Platform Collapse
A threat actor may have flawless OpSec, but the infrastructure they rely on almost never
does.
By measuring the microsecond drift in TCP sequence timestamps or HTTP Date
response headers over hundreds of connections across Tor, the system builds a 
hardware clock-skew profile.
This profile can be cross-referenced against candidate clearnet servers: two
machines sharing an identical, highly specific microsecond-per-day drift signature
are likely the same physical silicon.
Uptime / Downtime Co-Occurrence:
Bulletproof hosting providers (e.g., in Moldova, Russia, Netherlands) periodically
suffer fiber cuts, power outages, DDoS attacks, or maintenance windows.
When the target .onion goes offline, the platform checks global BGP routing
tables and Censys telemetry to see which clearnet Autonomous System Numbers
(ASNs) or subnets dropped offline at the exact same minute. Over 3–4 outages, the
intersection points to the physical data center.
How did they pay the hosting provider?
Bulletproof hosts advertise "Pay with Bitcoin/Crypto."
The host's automated billing system (e.g., WHMCS) generates a unique crypto
invoice address for that server rental.
When law enforcement executes an operation against the hosting provider or
intercepts its payment processor, the incoming transaction used to rent the specific
server hosting the .onion hidden service is uncovered.
That funding wallet can be traced back to its initial point of origin (an off-chain fiat
gateway, a peer-to-peer broker, or an exchange).
Threat actors operate on third-party forums (Dread, Exploit) or marketplaces.
Marketplace Seizure: When law enforcement (e.g., FBI, Europol, CBI) seizes the central
marketplace server (as occurred with Silk Road, AlphaBay, Hydra, and Bohemia):
Unencrypted internal server databases reveal internal private messages, buyer
delivery addresses, internal escrow ledger records, and server access logs.

=== PAGE 83 ===
Vector 5: The Law of Operational Security Decay (Entropy)
Flawless OpSec is mentally exhausting. To maintain absolute anonymity, a human being must be
100% disciplined, 24 hours a day, 365 days a year. The investigator only needs them to fail once.
Common mistakes that eventually occur:
Summary: What to Tell the Jury
If an evaluator asks: "What if the criminal is a genius who makes zero mistakes, uses Monero,
and leaves no misconfigurations?"
Even if the vendor’s public handle was clean, the seized database reveals internal
deposit logs, unencrypted communication with market admins, and operational
notes.
The Escrow / Arbiter Compromise: In an escrow dispute, the vendor must communicate
with a third-party moderator. The vendor’s OpSec depends on the moderator not being an
undercover investigator or an informant.
The Direct SSH Connection: An administrator forgets to switch on their local Tor proxy or
VPN before opening an SSH/RDP session to administer their backend server. A single
direct packet logged in /var/log/auth.log exposes their real IP.
1.
Clearnet Lookup from the Server: The administrator logs into their server via Tor, but runs
a script (e.g., git clone, curl, or an update script) that doesn't route through Tor. The
server connects directly to a clearnet repository or resolves DNS through a public resolver,
recording the connection.
2.
The Clearnet Blockchain Query: The actor receives Monero or Bitcoin and wants to check
if the transaction confirmed. Instead of running a local full node, they paste the
transaction hash or address into a public clearnet explorer (like Blockchain.com or
Mempool.space) from their home Wi-Fi or phone.
3.
Behavioral Slip: Months into operating, the actor posts a personal detail on a forum (a
time they wake up, a local holiday they observe, a slang term, a grievance with a local
utility, or an identical username used on a gaming forum a decade ago).
4.
Response:
"If an actor maintains perfect passive OpSec, our platform will
not hallucinate an attribution. It assigns a zero-confidence
baseline and creates an unlinked target profile. But in cyber
investigations, passive scanning is only stage one.
A criminal cannot make money while remaining completely inert. The
moment they sell goods, they must deliver digital or physical
payloads that leak compiler and filesystem metadata. They must pay
for hosting infrastructure, leaving a billing trail. They must

4.
Response:
"If an actor maintains perfect passive OpSec, our platform will
not hallucinate an attribution. It assigns a zero-confidence
baseline and creates an unlinked target profile. But in cyber
investigations, passive scanning is only stage one.
A criminal cannot make money while remaining completely inert. The
moment they sell goods, they must deliver digital or physical
payloads that leak compiler and filesystem metadata. They must pay
for hosting infrastructure, leaving a billing trail. They must
=== PAGE 84 ===
communicate with counterparties whose OpSec is flawed. And they
are subject to hardware side-channels like clock-skew and downtime
correlation.
Our platform automates passive discovery where OpSec errors exist,
and where they do not, it acts as the forensic repository that
ingests active investigative artifacts (canary tokens, payload
metadata, seized marketplace dumps) to resolve the entity when
operational discipline inevitably breaks."
ANSWER ME THE QUESTION S
1) WHERE THE CRIME OF BEING COMMITED IS IT PUBLIC NET
OR DARK NET OR BOTH
2)IF DARK NET HOW DOES SOLUTION HANDLE IT
1. Where Is the Crime Being Committed? Is It Public Net, Darknet, or Both?
The crime is almost always BOTH (Hybrid / Cross-Boundary Crime).
Cybercrime rarely exists in an isolated bubble. In real-world investigations handled by agencies
like the MHA / I4C, crimes fall into three distinct operational topologies:
 
[ Topology A: Darknet Native ]       [ Topology B: Cross-Boundary Extortion ]     [ T
 Crime: Illegal Bazaar                 Crime: Ransomware / Data Theft               
 Forum: Dread, Exploit                 Target: Clearnet Corporate/Govt              
 Committed: 100% on Dark Web           Ransom Portal: Dark Web (.onion)             
Darknet-Native Crimes: The illicit transaction takes place entirely within hidden services
(e.g., buying zero-day exploit payloads, hiring ransomware-as-a-service affiliates,
purchasing illicit weapons/narcotics, or trading leaked government databases on forums
like Dread or Exploit).
Cross-Boundary Crimes (The Most Common): The attack vector hits the public net
(phishing email, unpatched clearnet VPN exploit, banking malware), but the threat actors
coordinate, negotiate, and leak stolen victim data on a darknet .onion site (e.g.,
LockBit, BlackCat, or RansomHub leak blogs).
Clearnet Frauds with Darknet Cashout: Everyday cyber frauds (digital arrest scams, task-
based investment frauds, mule accounts) happen on the public net via WhatsApp,
Telegram, and fake banking apps. However, the money-laundering syndicates transfer the

communicate with counterparties whose OpSec is flawed. And they
are subject to hardware side-channels like clock-skew and downtime
correlation.
Our platform automates passive discovery where OpSec errors exist,
and where they do not, it acts as the forensic repository that
ingests active investigative artifacts (canary tokens, payload
metadata, seized marketplace dumps) to resolve the entity when
operational discipline inevitably breaks."

⚖  Self-Counter: Where This Fails / Limitations
2. If Darknet, How Does the Solution Handle It?
When the crime, the criminal, and the illicit services operate entirely on Tor, the platform
addresses the challenge through four synchronized layers:
stolen fiat into crypto (USDT-TRC20) and push it through darknet mixing services,
unlicensed OTC brokers, and cross-chain bridges.
If a cyber fraud remains 100% on the clearnet (e.g., local SIM boxes, UPI
phishing, money transferred to an Indian mule bank account and withdrawn at
an ATM in Jamtara or Mewat), a dark web platform is completely useless.
You must not pretend this system solves local clearnet telecom frauds. It is
strictly engineered for cases where the threat actor, the infrastructure, the
data leak, or the financial laundering hops intersect with the Tor/crypto
ecosystem.
 
                   ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
                   │             DARKNET OPERATION DETECTED                  │
                   └───────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────┘
                                               │
        ┌──────────────────────┬───────────────┴───────────────┬────────────────────
        ▼                       ▼                                ▼                     
[Autonomous Crawling]   [Infra De-anonymization]    [Crypto Entity Resolution]   [AI 
• Stem + Tor SOCKS5     • /server-status leaks      • RFC 4880 PGP parsing       • Wr
• PoW / Captcha bypass  • JARM & Favicon hashing    • UTXO Common-Input spend    • Si
• MinIO snapshot cache  • Censys/Shodan correlation • Mixer taint detection      • BE
Autonomous Continuous Gathering: The platform does not wait for manual input. A
Python Stem engine orchestrates headless browsers (Playwright Stealth) over Tor proxies (
socks5h://127.0.0.1:9050), dynamically cycling circuits to scrape forums, vendor
posts, and leak directories while handling rate limits and Proof-of-Work (PoW) captchas.
1.
Infrastructure De-anonymization (Unmasking the Origin Server): Tor hidden services
hide backend IPs, but the web servers powering them frequently leak configuration data.
The platform scans for:
2.
Misconfigured status endpoints (/server-status, /server-info, .git/, 
.env) that display raw internal/clearnet IP connection tables.
SSL/TLS certificates configured with clearnet domains (Subject Alternative Names).

=== PAGE 86 ===
⚖  Self-Counter: Where This Fails / Limitations
3. If the Victim Is on Public Net and the Criminal Has Gone Underground
(Darknet), What's the Solution?
This is the standard Ransomware / Data Extortion / Offshore Laundering scenario. The victim
operates on the clearnet, but the perpetrator directs them to an .onion site or withdraws
proceeds into the dark web.
Unique TLS stack implementations via 62-character JARM signatures and binary 
Favicon MurmurHash3 values, which are cross-queried against Censys and Shodan
to identify matching clearnet servers.
Cryptographic & Financial Knowledge Graph (Neo4j):3.
Extracts 40-character PGP Master Fingerprints (RFC 4880), rejecting spoofable 8-
byte short IDs.
Applies the Common-Input Ownership Heuristic on Bitcoin/EVM ledgers to cluster
multiple unhosted addresses into a single threat-actor wallet cluster.
AI Stylometric Authorship Attribution: When an actor discards their handles and keys,
the system vectorizes their writing samples using 400+ Writeprints features and fine-
tuned Siamese neural networks, matching their subconscious linguistic patterns across
different darknet platforms.
4.
If a darknet operator deploys a hardened Tor v3 service (no status pages,
standard Nginx configs that generate generic JARM hashes, strict local
firewall), transacts exclusively in Monero (XMR), and runs all text through an
LLM rewriter or posts 5-word messages, passive scanning hits a mathematical wall.
In that specific scenario, the tool cannot magically uncover the IP. It can only
flag the target as HARDENED_ZERO_SIGNAL and wait for active operational
errors (e.g., accidental clearnet lookups, hosting billing subpoena trails, or
marketplace seizures).
[ Victim on Clearnet ] ── ►  (Infected / Defrauded) ── ►  [ Ransom Note / Extortion Link 
                                                             │
                                                             ▼
                                              [ Input into ShadowTrace ]
                                                             │
            ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────
            ▼                                                                        
  [ Financial Path Tracing ]                                                        
  • Victim's payment TX hash ingested                                               
  • Heuristic-driven BFS forward walk

The Step-by-Step Investigation Workflow:
⚖  Self-Counter: Where This Fails / Limitations
  • Stops at Nearest VASP (Centralized Exchange)                                    
            │                                                                        
            ▼                                                                        
[ Section 94 BNSS Freezing Order to Exchange ]                                  [ Leg
Pivot on Public-Net Artifacts: The Investigating Officer (IO) inputs the specific
breadcrumbs provided by the victim: the extortion .onion URL, the demand letter, the
contact handle (Jabber/Telegram/Tox), the cryptocurrency payment address, or the
malware sample.
1.
Reverse-Correlating the .onion Infrastructure:2.
Ransomware gangs frequently host their negotiation portals or leak sites on servers
that share infrastructure with clearnet mirrors or developer testing portals.
The platform probes the negotiation URL for JARM/TLS and favicon hashes. If the
developer deployed the same code on a clearnet VPS for testing, Censys/Shodan
correlation instantly reveals the real IP and the hosting provider (e.g., HostKey, OVH,
Selectel).
Forward Financial Tracing to Nearest VASP (Asset Recovery):3.
The victim paid cryptocurrency (e.g., Bitcoin or USDT). The criminal moves it through
3 to 7 intermediary "hop" wallets to shake off basic observers.
The platform uses a bounded-pruning Breadth-First Search (BFS) forward-path
traversal, tracking the movement of tainted funds across unhosted wallets while
filtering out CoinJoin mixers.
The moment an intermediary wallet deposits into a Centralized Virtual Asset
Service Provider (VASP) (such as Binance, CoinDCX, or Kraken), the platform
pinpoints the individual user deposit address.
Statutory Enforcement Action: The platform automatically generates a statutory freezing
and disclosure requisition under Section 94 of the Bharatiya Nagarik Suraksha Sanhita
(BNSS), 2023, ready for direct dispatch to the exchange's legal cell via the SAHYOG Portal
to freeze the KYC-linked bank/fiat payout.
4.
If the criminal demands payment directly in Monero (XMR) or the victim uses an
automated atomic-swap service (BTC  XMR) before paying, on-chain forward
tracking breaks completely.
RingCT and stealth addresses prevent BFS wallet traversal. In that case,
financial tracing stops, and the investigation must rely entirely on host
→

=== PAGE 88 ===
4. What if Both Victim and Criminal Are on the Dark Web, Fraud Occurs There,
and the Victim Files a Complaint?
Scenario: A user (whistleblower, buyer, or even an individual purchasing illegal/grey-market
items) gets exit-scammed or defrauded inside a darknet forum (e.g., Dread) or an .onion
marketplace, and approaches a cyber crime unit or lodges a complaint via the 1930 Helpline /
NCRP.
The Step-by-Step Investigation Workflow:
forensics of the victim's compromised network (memory dumps, C2 communication
logs) and infrastructure unmasking of the .onion negotiation portal.

Ingestion of Darknet Evidence Artifacts:1.
The investigator collects the transaction hash, the vendor's advertised public PGP
key, the vendor handle, chat transcripts, and the specific marketplace URL.
Cross-Marketplace Entity Expansion (Neo4j):2.
Dark web vendors rarely operate on just one market; they diversify risk across
multiple sites (e.g., Dread, Archetyp, Bohemia).

Dark web vendors rarely operate on just one market; they diversify risk across
multiple sites (e.g., Dread, Archetyp, Bohemia).
The platform searches its pre-indexed Knowledge Graph:

=== PAGE 89 ===
⚖  Self-Counter: Where This Fails / Critical Legal & Technical Roadblocks
Did this vendor use the same 40-character Master PGP Key Fingerprint on
another market?
Did they advertise an alternative contact handle (Jabber, Tox, Telegram)?
If they changed their handle, does their listing text match a known vendor
profile via AI Stylometry (Writeprints + Siamese Transformer)?
Differentiating Direct Fraud vs. Escrow Fraud:3.
Case A (Direct Vendor Payment): The victim sent crypto directly to the vendor's
wallet. The platform traces that wallet address forward through the blockchain until it
hits an exchange or an off-ramp service.
Case B (Marketplace Escrow Fraud / Exit Scam): The victim paid into the
marketplace's central escrow wallet. Here, the vendor didn't steal the money directly
—the marketplace operators absconded with all escrowed funds. The platform flags
the market's master hot-wallet clusters and tracks the administrative consolidation
sweeps.
Phishing Site & Clone Detection:4.
A massive percentage of "darknet-on-darknet fraud" is actually Tor Phishing Clones.
Threat actors create deceptive .onion links that look identical to legitimate
marketplaces to harvest credentials and deposit coins.
The platform probes the fraudulent .onion site. Phishing operators run cheap,
mass-deployed web proxies that almost always leak their clearnet origin IPs through
missing reverse-proxy protections, default server headers, or identical SSL certificate
serials.
1. The "Unclean Hands" / Legal Admissibility Paradox: If the victim was on the
dark web attempting to purchase contraband (drugs, weapons, illicit data,
malware) and was defrauded, the victim has admitted to an attempted criminal
offense under Indian law (e.g., NDPS Act, Arms Act, BNS). Police protocol
requires initiating legal action or an inquiry against the complainant as
well.
2. The Off-Chain Internal Ledger Problem: If the fraud happened inside a
centralized dark web marketplace between two registered account
balances (an internal database credit shift), there is zero trace on any public
blockchain. The money never moved on-chain; it moved inside a private
MySQL/Postgres database on the market's server. Unless international law
enforcement seizes the physical marketplace server and its unencrypted
database, attributing an internal off-chain balance theft is
mathematically impossible from the outside.

B, which praises Handle C, which transacts only with Handle A).

graph. Closed trust loops with zero external validation are programmatically flagged
as SYNTHETIC_SYBIL_RING and pruned from the attribution score.

Evasion Vector 4: Algorithmic Multi-Chain Liquidity Shuffling (DeFi Laundering)
The Adversary's AI Playbook:
How the Platform Catches You via Public Data:
04:00, 09:00, 15:00, and 22:00, and answers basic buyer questions

Separation of Asynchronous vs. Interactive Latency:1.
The platform categorizes public posts into Broadcasts (marketplace listings, initial
forum announcements) and Interactive Exchanges (thread disputes, multi-turn reply
chains, live support threads).
Scheduled cron-jitter works for broadcasts, but fails during rapid human back-and-
forth exchanges. When a critical deal or controversy occurs, the human behind the
bot steps in. The system filters for inter-arrival response intervals (
), isolating the real operator's active consciousness from background automated
queues.
< 180 seconds
Blockchain Ledger Timestamps (The Unfakable Clock): You can schedule a forum post,
but when you accept a customer payment and sweep the funds, the confirmation is
sealed into an immutable block header on the public blockchain.
2.
By correlating transaction execution timestamps with public block generation times
across hundreds of transactions, the platform reconstructs your operational
settlement window, bypassing your fake forum delays entirely.
"I don't touch centralized mixers or clean Bitcoin transactions.
The moment illicit USDT-TRC20 lands in my wallet, an AI script
splits it into 20 micro-transactions, bridges it across Stargate
to Arbitrum, swaps it through Uniswap v3 liquidity pools into ETH,
converts it to Solana via Wormhole, and distributes it across 50
disposable unhosted wallets. You cannot track a peeling chain
across 4 different virtual machines."
Public Smart-Contract Event Logs: Blockchains are not black boxes; every decentralized
exchange (DEX) and bridge emits deterministic public events (Swap, Sync, 
LogScriptCall).
1.
The platform’s ingestion engine parses EVM and TVM event logs directly. When your
script calls a bridge contract, the destination recipient address is written directly into
the public transaction payload on the destination chain. The platform picks up the
thread on the other side.

It maps the liquidity paths across the bridges, ignoring unrelated gas dust (

It tracks the temporal window: your automated scripts swap and bridge within

The Inevitable Cash-Out Chokepoint (The Nearest VASP):3.
This is where your AI plan breaks. You cannot buy real-world real estate, luxury
goods, or physical cash with wrapped liquidity tokens on a smart contract.
Eventually, the money must be liquidated. The funds must flow to a Peer-to-Peer
(P2P) merchant, an OTC cash desk, or a centralized exchange.
The moment those fragmented tokens hit a deposit address that sweeps into an
exchange hot wallet (e.g., Binance, CoinDCX, Bybit), the platform flags the Nearest
VASP Customer Deposit Address.
A statutory requisition notice under Section 94 BNSS hits the exchange compliance
team via the SAHYOG Portal, freezing the KYC-verified account before the fiat
payout completes.
"My server has no open /server-status, no .git, and no exposed 
.env. My Tor v3 hidden service runs inside an ephemeral Docker
container. Every 48 hours, my script destroys the VPS, spins up a
new server at a different provider with a randomized IP,
regenerates my Nginx configs with randomized TLS cipher suites,
and shifts the .onion front-end. Your Censys, Shodan, and JARM
signatures are tracking a server that died yesterday."
Certificate Transparency (CT) Logs (Permanent Public Ledger):1.
Even if you rotate your server every 48 hours, if your deployment script ever requests
an SSL certificate for a staging clearnet domain or uses a shared dynamic DNS
service, that certificate is permanently logged in public Certificate Transparency
(CT) logs (e.g., crt.sh).

=== PAGE 95 ===
The Reality Check: Where Does the Adversary Win?
To remain a rigorous cybersecurity analysis, here are the exact mathematical limits where an AI-
augmented criminal successfully evades detection:
The platform monitors CT logs continuously. CT logs are append-only and
cryptographically auditable—you cannot delete your historical deployment
footprints.
BGP Autonomous System Routing (Bulletproof Subnets Are Finite):2.
You can change your IP address a hundred times, but mainstream hosting providers
(AWS, Google Cloud, DigitalOcean, Hetzner) actively seize cybercrime
infrastructure.
To keep an illicit service online, you are mathematically forced to deploy inside 
Bulletproof Hosting Providers (e.g., in specific offshore, uncooperative ASNs).
There are only a few dozen functional bulletproof ASNs globally. When your server
rotates, it moves from one IP in AS44552 to another IP in the exact same subnet
block or peered infrastructure. Neo4j models this as an infrastructure cluster,
correlating the repeated deployment behavior to the same entity.
The Human Invariant: The SSH Deployment Bastion:3.
Your AI script does not run in thin air; it is triggered from a central repository, a local
controller laptop, or an administrative command server.
If that central deployment controller connects to the hosting providers to issue API
tokens or deploy configurations, the hosting provider's billing and access logs
capture the master origin footprint.

The Core Conclusion
An AI-augmented threat actor can randomize their syntax, rotate their servers, and shuffle their
crypto across bridges. But to execute a successful cybercrime enterprise, they cannot
eliminate the economic objective.
The moment they interact with counterparties, deploy functional payloads, and attempt to
extract real-world value, they leave immutable public footprints. The platform does not rely on
catching every single move; it tracks the irreversible physical and financial chokepoints where
evasion is mathematically impossible.

Detection
Fully Air-Gapped, Non-
Commercial Operations
BLOCKED If an actor does not cash out and
operates strictly within an
isolated darknet enclave without
external dependencies, there
are no financial or infrastructure
bridges to clearnet reality.

                               ▼
      [ Correlation Engine: Asymmetric Rules-Governed Scorer ]
           (Deterministic Hard Overrides vs. Capped Soft Signals)

         (Temporal Timeline Slider + Legal Verification Gateway)
                               │
                               ▼
       [ Multi-Format Forensic Exporters: CSV / STIX 2.1 JSON / PDF ]
Autonomous Ingestion: Containerized worker daemons route headless browser sessions
through isolated Tor SOCKS5 proxies (socks5h://127.0.0.1:9050). The crawler
executes client-side WebAssembly (WASM) and Proof-of-Work (PoW) scripts, extracts
session authentication cookies into a shared Redis vault, and harvests marketplace
listings, vendor profiles, and forum discussions. If a hidden service drops offline, the
engine falls back to an immutable snapshot replay cache in MinIO.
1.
DOCX+ 2
Parallel Feature Extraction: Payloads are ingested via Redis and processed
asynchronously by Celery workers across three specialized pipelines: infrastructure recon,
cryptographic/financial mapping, and natural language processing.
2.
DOCX
Graph Mapping & Correlation: Extracted entities populate a Neo4j property graph.
Deterministic triggers automatically merge aliases sharing hard cryptographic anchors,
while probabilistic signals are evaluated via an asymmetric scoring engine.
3.
DOCX+ 1
Analyst Inspection & Human-in-the-Loop Validation: Investigators evaluate candidate
links using side-by-side comparative diffs, inspect temporal migrations via a timeline
slider, and confirm or reject identity mergers with mandatory audit logging.
4.
DOCX
Multi-Format Dissemination: Confirmed intelligence is packaged into CSV Indicator-of-
Compromise (IOC) feeds, STIX 2.1-compliant JSON threat intelligence bundles, and signed,
court-defensible PDF dossiers.

B. Multi-Marketplace Entity Linkage & Financial Resolution
Application-Layer Misconfigurations:
Probes hidden services for exposed /server-status and /server-info
endpoints. It parses Apache connection tables to extract client IP addresses, internal
virtual hosts, and upstream backend reverse proxies.
 DOCX+ 1
Checks for directory traversal disclosures (.git/HEAD, .env, phpinfo.php,
staging backup archives) to locate hardcoded clearnet endpoints, database
credentials, and internal paths.
 DOCX
Cryptographic & Asset Fingerprinting:
SSL/TLS Leaf Extraction: Scrapes SSL/TLS certificates presented over Tor. Extracts
SHA-256 certificate digests, Common Names (CN), and Subject Alternative Names
(SAN) that unintentionally reference clearnet fully qualified domain names (FQDNs).
DOCX+ 1
JARM Signatures: Transmits 10 specialized TLS Client Hello packets to generate a 62-
character cryptographic hash of the underlying TLS stack, mapping unique cipher-
suite ordering and OpenSSL compile parameters.
Favicon Hashes: Computes MurmurHash3 against the binary /favicon.ico asset.
DOCX
Clearnet Correlation & CDN Disambiguation:
Queries Censys and Shodan APIs using the extracted fingerprints:
Query: services.jarm: [JARM] ∧ services.tls.certificates.leaf_
Discovered clearnet IPs are cross-referenced with BGP routing tables. Addresses
belonging to CDN/DDoS-mitigation ASNs (Cloudflare, Akamai, Fastly, DDoS-Guard)
are tagged in Neo4j as (:EdgeProxy). Non-CDN IPs matching both JARM and SSL
signatures are flagged as candidate (:OriginServer) backends.
Dual-Mode Legal Operational Gate:
Passive Mode (Default): Infrastructure matches rely exclusively on pre-indexed
internet databases (Censys/Shodan). No packets are transmitted to candidate
clearnet hosts.
Active Probing Mode (Warrant-Gated): Direct verification (sending an HTTP request
with the target's Host header to a candidate backend IP to confirm response parity)
is locked behind role-based controls. It requires a valid case authorization identifier
and a senior authority approval flag before execution.
 DOCX

C. Translation-Resilient AI Stylometry & Behavioral Profiling
RFC 4880 PGP Parsing: Ingests ASCII-armored PGP public keys, programmatically
extracting the 160-bit SHA-1 or 256-bit SHA-2 Master Key Fingerprint, creation timestamps,
and user ID strings (Name <email@domain>). Rejects easily spoofed 8-byte short IDs,
anchoring nodes strictly on cryptographic master fingerprints.
 DOCX+ 1
UTXO Transaction Graphing & Mixer Taint Handling:
Ingests cryptocurrency addresses via RPC nodes.
Common-Input Ownership Heuristic: Combines multiple input addresses spent
within a single Bitcoin transaction into a unified entity cluster using union-find logic.
DOCX
CoinJoin / Mixer Taint Guard: Scans incoming transactions for mixer characteristics (
 inputs and  identical-denomination outputs, e.g.,  BTC or  BTC). If
detected, automated clustering is halted, and the transaction is mapped to an
isolated (:MixerHop) edge to prevent false-positive entity mergers.
≥ 5 ≥ 5 0.05 0.1
Monero (XMR) Off-Chain Tracking: Acknowledges base-layer cryptographic boundaries
(RingCT, stealth addresses). Monero is tracked through off-chain correlation:
Ingests public vendor addresses, subaddresses, and recurring payment IDs
published in forum threads.
Correlates vendor deposit requirements with public ledger block confirmation
timestamps ( ) and transaction volume ranges.
< Δt
Classifies Bitcoin nodes as HEURISTIC_CLUSTERED and Monero nodes strictly as 
DETERMINISTIC_EXACT_MATCH or TEMPORAL_CORRELATION.
// Cypher: Cross-Marketplace Identity Resolution on Shared PGP Key
MATCH (h1:ForumHandle)-[:SIGNS_WITH]->(k:PGPKey)<-[:SIGNS_WITH]-(h2:ForumHandle)
WHERE h1 <> h2 AND NOT (h1)-[:ALIASED_WITH]-(h2)
MERGE (ta:ThreatActor {master_id: k.fingerprint})
MERGE (h1)-[:OPERATED_BY]->(ta)
MERGE (h2)-[:OPERATED_BY]->(ta)
SET ta.last_correlation_date = datetime()
RETURN ta.master_id, count(DISTINCT h1) AS LinkedHandles;
Adversarial AI Sanitization Gate: Forum posts are routed through Fast-DetectGPT and
token log-rank analysis to identify machine-translated or LLM-rewritten text. If text
displays unnaturally uniform log-rank characteristics, the engine flags it as 
Cypher  

subaddresses, keeps messages to minimal bullet points, and leaves no misconfigurations:
Sybil Bot Networks: The actor deploys 50 automated LLM accounts to create artificial
forum reputations.
2.

Neo4j PageRank centrality algorithms. Trust links require either verifiable on-chain
multi-sig transactions or valid RFC 4880 PGP signatures from established master
keys.

diurnal sleep curves.

scheduled posts) from interactive exchanges (support threads, dispute responses
with latency ). It cross-references operational activity with
blockchain block-header timestamps, which cannot be spoofed by artificial forum
delays.

infrastructure clusters in Neo4j.

How the Platform Catches You via Public Data:
The Entropy Trap (Fast-DetectGPT & Log-Rank Analysis): LLMs select tokens based
on constrained probability distributions. Human writing exhibits high burstiness and
structural variance; synthetic writing is statistically uniform. The platform evaluates
the log-rank perplexity of ingested forum posts. The moment a text block displays
synthetic uniformity, it is flagged as ADVERSARIALLY_SANITIZED.

BERTopic Intent Graphs: The platform extracts these domain-specific technical

The Scamster's Tactic:"I use autonomous LLM bots to spin up 50 accounts across Dread,
Exploit, and Telegram. They write glowing reviews of my illicit shop, dispute accusations,
and manufacture fake trade volumes to overwhelm your knowledge graph."
How the Platform Catches You via Public Data:
Graph Centrality & Closed-Loop Pruning: Organic human communities on the dark
web form scale-free networks centered around long-standing community arbiters.
Synthetic bot swarms inevitably form closed, highly dense subgraphs (Bot A
transacts with Bot B, which validates Bot C, which endorses the Scamster). The Neo4j
graph engine runs PageRank and Eigenvector Centrality algorithms, automatically
identifying and pruning isolated, self-referential trust loops.
1.
The Cryptographic Proof Requirement: The platform assigns zero trust weight to
unverified textual forum claims. An edge in the knowledge graph requires:
2.
DOCX
An on-chain public transaction on a public ledger, or
1
1
A cryptographically valid signature generated by an aged RFC 4880 PGP Master
Key Fingerprint.
2
2 DOCX
Fifty automated bot handles with empty wallets and newly generated
unverified keys collapse into an isolated, untrusted cluster.
3
3
The Scamster's Tactic:"I set up an automated cron worker with random Poisson delays. It
releases posts, bumps listings, and answers basic inquiries at 03:00, 09:00, 15:00, and
21:00 UTC. My public timestamp distribution looks like a 24/7 flat line. You cannot
determine my timezone."
How the Platform Catches You via Public Data:
Interactive vs. Asynchronous Latency Filtering:1.
The platform separates Broadcast Events (forum threads, marketplace product
uploads) from Interactive Events (multi-turn dispute threads, real-time
negotiation responses).
1
1
Scheduled background scripts handle broadcasts, but when a buyer challenges
an order or an escrow mediator threatens to release funds, the real human
operator must intervene. The engine isolates interaction pairs with response
2

Graph Centrality & Closed-Loop Pruning: Organic human communities on the dark
web form scale-free networks centered around long-standing community arbiters.
Synthetic bot swarms inevitably form closed, highly dense subgraphs (Bot A
transacts with Bot B, which validates Bot C, which endorses the Scamster). The Neo4j
graph engine runs PageRank and Eigenvector Centrality algorithms, automatically
identifying and pruning isolated, self-referential trust loops.

The Public Blockchain Clock: You can delay a forum post, but when a customer
deposits crypto and you sweep it, the transaction confirmation is permanently
stamped into a public block header. The platform extracts the block confirmation
timestamps of your financial sweeps across weeks of activity, reconstructing your
operational settlement window regardless of synthetic forum post delays.

pools into stablecoins, and converts it to Solana. Your simple wallet-matching rules will
fail."
How the Platform Catches You via Public Data:
Smart Contract Event Logs Are Public: Decentralized exchanges (DEXs) and cross-
chain bridges are public state machines. Every time an automated script invokes a
smart contract, public EVM/TVM logs emit deterministic events (Swap, Sync, 
BridgeDeposit). The platform decodes contract transaction parameters,
extracting the destination recipient wallet directly from bridge payload parameters.

5. Evasion: Ephemeral VPS Swarms & Anti-Reconnaissance Hardening
Act III: The Hard Reality Check (Where Does the Scamster Win?)
A real-world intelligence platform must recognize the absolute mathematical boundaries where
passive scanning cannot succeed:
The moment the algorithm deposits into a customer-specific deposit forwarder
that sweeps into an exchange hot wallet, the platform tags the Nearest VASP
Customer Account.
3
3
It generates an automated Section 94 BNSS Freezing and KYC Disclosure
Order ready for dispatch via the SAHYOG Portal, freezing the KYC-verified
account before the fiat payout executes.
4
4
The Scamster's Tactic:"My Tor hidden service runs inside an ephemeral, hardened Docker
container. I have disabled /server-status, removed .git, and randomized my Nginx
banners. Every 48 hours, my script destroys the server, spins up a fresh VPS under a new IP
address, and points the .onion front-end to it. Your Censys/Shodan queries will only find
a dead IP."
How the Platform Catches You via Public Data:
Public Certificate Transparency (CT) Logs: Even if you destroy your VPS every two
days, if your deployment automation ever requests a TLS certificate for a staging
domain, an API endpoint, or a backup interface, that request is immutably recorded
in public, append-only Certificate Transparency (CT) logs (such as crt.sh). The
platform queries CT logs to extract historical staging subdomains (
staging.api.target.com) that point to your permanent administration IPs.
1.
BGP Autonomous System Routing (The Bulletproof ASN Chokepoint):2.
Major hosting providers (AWS, Azure, DigitalOcean, Hetzner) proactively shut
down illicit services.
1
1
To keep a dark web shop online, you are mathematically forced to use 
Bulletproof Hosting Providers (specialized, uncooperative hosts in select
jurisdictions).
2
2
There are only a few dozen functional bulletproof ASNs globally. When your
deployment script destroys a server and buys another, it almost always deploys
within the exact same subnet block or peered Autonomous System Number
(ASN). Neo4j clusters these infrastructure deployments together, proving they
originate from the same operational provisioning pipeline.
3

Act IV: The Law of Operational Security Entropy (Why the Defense Wins)
The fundamental asymmetry of cybercrime investigations is defined by OpSec Entropy:
By fusing Infrastructure Discovery, Blockchain Financial Graphs, and AI Stylometric &
Semantic Profiling, the platform continuously collects the breadcrumbs. When the criminal's
discipline inevitably breaks, the knowledge graph links the new alias directly to the real-world
individual.

1. The Undercover Cop Problem: Preventing Blue-on-Blue Attribution
If an undercover law enforcement officer (UC) operates on the dark web (posing as a vendor,
buyer, or broker on forums like Dread or Exploit), a naive automated crawler will scrape their
handle, index their PGP key, log their test wallets, and flag them as a high-threat criminal.
To prevent this "blue-on-blue" false positive, the platform implements a Cryptographic
Deconfliction Vault (LEA-FAR: Law Enforcement Asset Register). DOCX
[ Ingested Entity (Handle / PGP / Wallet) ]
                    │
                    ▼
      [ Salted SHA-256 Hash Comparison ] ◄ ───  [ Encrypted LEA Deconfliction Vault ]
                    │
        ┌───────────┴───────────┐
        ▼                        ▼
   [ Match Found ]         [ No Match ]
        │                        │
        ▼                        ▼
• Tag: PROTECTED_ASSET      • Standard Pipeline
• Suppress Public Scoring   • Ingest into Neo4j
• Mask from Junior Analysts • Normal Attribution
• Alert Operation Lead
The Hash-Blind Deconfliction Vault:
Undercover operational assets (burner handles, operational PGP fingerprints, test
cryptocurrency addresses, and dedicated proxy IP egresses) are registered in an
isolated, encrypted table in PostgreSQL.
 DOCX
To protect ongoing undercover operations from insider leaks, these assets are stored
as salted SHA-256 cryptographic hashes. The crawling engine hashes every
extracted identifier and checks for collision against the vault.
Operational Cloaking & Role-Based Segregation:
When a scraped indicator matches a registered undercover asset, the system halts
automatic scoring and suppression kicks in.
The node is tagged with metadata: STATUS: PROTECTED_LE_OPERATIONAL_ASSET.
DOCX
The entity is automatically masked from standard Junior Analyst workspace views to
prevent burning an active undercover officer's cover across jurisdictions.

Detailed Stage Breakdown: Silk Road
3. Scamster Reconciles Their Mistake Mid-Way: How the Platform Prevents
Evasion
A common real-world scenario occurs when an actor makes an operational error (e.g., exposes
an Apache status page for two hours, reuses a personal PGP key, or posts a Discord/Telegram
handle), realizes their mistake, deletes the post, rotates their keys, and hardens their server.
The platform neutralizes retroactive clean-up through four persistence mechanisms:
│                          │                                         │  (HIGH CONFIDENC
└──────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────────────┴────────────────
Stage 1: Infrastructure De-anonymization (Score: 0.96)
The Historical Reality: The Silk Road web server leaked its true physical backend IP (
193.107.86.49, hosted at FDI in Iceland) because the login/CAPTCHA script
transmitted raw web traffic directly over the public internet rather than looping
exclusively through the local Tor proxy interface (127.0.0.1).
Platform Performance: The active probing module identifies that the CAPTCHA asset
request does not route through the SOCKS5 proxy interface, logs the external socket
connection, and maps 193.107.86.49 directly as the (:OriginServer).
 DOCX
Stage 2: Cryptographic & Financial Knowledge Graph (Score: 0.92)
The Historical Reality: Ulbricht regularly consolidated commission fees from the Silk
Road escrow system into personal wallet addresses, eventually moving thousands of
Bitcoins directly onto his laptop without mixer hops.
Platform Performance: The UTXO Common-Input Ownership heuristic clusters the
multi-input consolidation transactions, linking the marketplace's primary fee-
collection addresses directly to Ulbricht's private wallet cluster.
 DOCX
Stage 3: AI Stylometry & Behavioral Profiling (Score: 0.84)
The Historical Reality: Before launching Silk Road, Ulbricht posted under the handle
"altoid" on Bitcointalk and Shroomery advertising the site, expressing specific
ideological viewpoints on Austrian economics. Later, as "Dread Pirate Roberts," his
long essays reflected identical philosophical and syntactic conventions.
Platform Performance: BERTopic identifies high-density semantic alignment around
Agorist/Libertarian economic terminology, while Siamese RoBERTa links the
structural phrasing between "altoid" and "DPR".
 DOCX
Stage 4: Final Attribution Score:0.95 (HIGH CONFIDENCE). Driven by direct clearnet IP
capture and non-mixer UTXO multi-input spends.

anchored in the graph.

(CT) logs (crt.sh)[cite: 1].

Statement description[cite: 1]. These components provide the autonomous collection,

active canary payloads, and automated Section 94 BNSS legal notices are tactical

"Enterprise Law Enforcement Operational Extensions" that bridge the gap between
academic software and field deployment.

Part 1: What if an Undercover Cop's ID is Leaked and a Threat Actor Misuses It as
a Shield?
If a threat actor compromises, buys, or impersonates an undercover law enforcement officer’s
(UC) dark web identity (their forum handle, compromised credentials, or private PGP key) to
commit crimes under the assumption that the police deconfliction vault will shield them, a
properly engineered system does not grant blanket immunity.
In law enforcement architecture, deconfliction is governed by Zero-Trust Operational
Monitoring:


Part 1: What if an Undercover Cop's ID is Leaked and a Threat Actor Misuses It as
a Shield?
If a threat actor compromises, buys, or impersonates an undercover law enforcement officer’s
(UC) dark web identity (their forum handle, compromised credentials, or private PGP key) to
commit crimes under the assumption that the police deconfliction vault will shield them, a
properly engineered system does not grant blanket immunity.
In law enforcement architecture, deconfliction is governed by Zero-Trust Operational
Monitoring:

=== PAGE 119 ===
1. AI Stylometric & Behavioral Anomaly Detection
An undercover officer and an external criminal do not share the same brain or subconscious
writing habits.
2. Access Telemetry & Operational Constraints
Undercover officers do not access dark web targets from arbitrary environments. Standard
operating procedures (SOPs) require UCs to operate through designated secure virtual
environments, specific hardware tokens, or controlled network gateways.
3. Time-Bounded and Case-Scoped Deconfliction
[ Compromised UC Asset Used on Dark Web ]
                   │
                   ▼
    [ Hash-Blind Deconfliction Vault ]
                   │
                   ├──  Match: "Protected Asset"
                   │
                   ▼
  [ Zero-Trust Behavioral Divergence Check ]
   ├──  1. Stylometric Drift (AI Stylometry detects different human author)
   ├──  2. Telemetry Mismatch (IP / Tor Guard / Client footprint anomaly)
   └──  3. Scope Violation (Activity outside authorized case boundaries)
                   │
                   ▼
    [ High-Priority Red Flag Dispatched ]
   ├──  Direct Alert to Special Operations Commander
   ├──  Immediate Revocation of Protected Status
   └──  Target Node Reclassified: "HOSTILE_TAKEOVER / ACTIVE_CRIMINAL"
The platform maintains a baseline Writeprints vector and Siamese Transformer
embedding of the authorized undercover officer’s historical communications.
 DOCX
When the hijacked account posts new listings, threats, or dispute responses, the NLP
engine computes the cosine distance against the officer's established profile.
 DOCX
If the stylometric similarity drops below threshold (e.g., sudden shift in vocabulary
richness, punctuation anomalies, function-word distribution, or language patterns), the
platform generates a Persona Drift Alert.
 DOCX
If the compromised handle suddenly connects or posts via an unauthorized Tor circuit
profile, a different client user-agent, or an unrecognized operational cadence, the system
flags a Telemetry Mismatch.

 DOCX

 DOCX

=== PAGE 120 ===
Deconfliction registrations are never permanent "get-out-of-jail-free" cards.
4. Dual-Key Supervisor Oversight (The Killswitch)
Deconfliction does not suppress or delete logs; it redirects them:
Part 2: Scope & Feasibility Strategy for Hackathons (SIH)
Do not attempt to code out-of-scope edge cases during the hackathon sprint. Doing so
introduces severe technical risk, dilutes your focus, and can cause you to lose points on the
official judging rubric.
Evaluation committees score teams against the official Problem Statement criteria. If an
autonomous crawler fails or graph export buttons break during a live evaluation because the
team spent 14 hours coding an undercover deconfliction vault or an active canary token
generator, the submission will be penalized.
To maintain engineering feasibility while showing enterprise-level thinking, use the 3-Tier
Hackathon Execution Framework:
Every entry in the Law Enforcement Asset Register is strictly scoped:
Time-Boxed: Valid only for specific operational windows (e.g., 
valid_until: 2026-09-30).
Domain-Boxed: Valid only on specific forums or operations (e.g., 
target_market: Dread).
If the compromised asset is used on an unapproved marketplace, for an unapproved
crime category (e.g., active ransomware extortion when the UC was only cleared for an
intelligence-gathering buy), the deconfliction rule automatically trips an alarm.
Any activity matching a registered undercover asset is mirrored in real time to the Special
Operations Commander / Case Supervisor console.
 DOCX
The supervisor knows what the undercover officer is tasked with doing hour-by-hour. If the
supervisor sees listings for stolen corporate databases or weapons that the officer never
logged in their official daily dispatch, the supervisor triggers an immediate Asset
Revocation Killswitch.
The asset's protected tag is stripped, the node is reclassified in Neo4j as 
HOSTILE_COMPROMISE, and the platform initiates an automated trace against the hijacker
.
 DOCX
DOCX

Tier 3: Edge Cases & Operational Extensions (Slides Only — Architectural
Defense)
Address the complex edge cases during the slide presentation and Q&A session:
How to Deliver This Strategy to the Evaluation Jury
When clicked, have the frontend trigger a pre-formatted, professional PDF export
that populates the suspect's wallet address, transaction hash, and target exchange
name into the legal notice format.
Why this works: It demonstrates a deep understanding of Indian cybercrime
enforcement workflows without having to build a live API connection to government
portals.
Include a dedicated slide titled: "Enterprise & Law Enforcement Operational
Safeguards."
Break down how the architecture addresses these challenges:
Undercover Protection: Hash-blind deconfliction vault and Zero-Trust stylometric
anomaly detection.
1.
LLM-Sanitized Text: Perplexity filtering via Fast-DetectGPT to downweight AI-
rewritten posts.
2.
DOCX
CoinJoin Mixers: Halting automatic wallet merges when transaction topology
indicates mixer usage.
3.
DOCX
Reverse Proxies: BGP ASN filtering to separate Cloudflare/Fastly edge proxies from
true origin servers.
4.
"Our prototype demonstrates the core technical capabilities demanded by the
Problem Statement: autonomous crawling, Tor misconfiguration unmasking,
Neo4j multi-marketplace graphing, AI stylometry, and multi-format evidence
reporting.
Beyond the baseline requirements, our architectural design
incorporates real-world law enforcement operational safeguards—
including zero-trust deconfliction for undercover personnel,
adversarial LLM sanitization detection, and statutory notice
alignment with the SAHYOG portal under Section 94 BNSS. We have
isolated these operational extensions to the enterprise deployment
phase, ensuring our core collection and attribution engine remains
reliable, scalable, and demo-ready today."
DOCX
